# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

Site Monitor — full-stack SSL/TLS certificate monitoring system. Spring Boot 4.1 / Java 25 backend serves a React 18 (Vite 5) SPA; PostgreSQL is the system of record. Single deployable: the React `dist/` is served as static content from the Spring Boot jar in prod. Single VERSION file (`./VERSION`) is authoritative for both backend (`pom.xml` uses `${revision}`; the Docker build passes `-Drevision=$(cat VERSION)`, local builds produce `0.0.0-local`) and frontend (`vite.config.js` reads it at build time).

The UI is bilingual TR/EN. **The default language is English** (product decision 2026-10-02): a user with no stored choice sees EN, and a chosen language is kept. The default is read in three places that must agree: `storedLang()` in `i18n/index.jsx`, `dateLocale()` in `i18n/dateLocale.js` (en-GB), and the `X-Lang` header in `api/client.js` (gates: `defaultLanguage.test.jsx`, `client-lang-header.test.js`). The backend `Msg.t` fallback stays `tr` for requests without a header (mails, jobs). Most identifiers, log messages, and admin-panel strings are Turkish — keep i18n keys in sync (see `i18n-parity.test.jsx`).

## Common commands

Java + Maven paths are user-specific: `JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Zulu\zulu-25`, Maven at `D:\portablePrograms\apache-maven-3.9.9\bin\mvn.cmd`. Backend health endpoint is `/health` (Actuator remapped, base-path is `/`).

### Backend (from `backend/`)
```
mvn spring-boot:run           # dev — runs on :8080
mvn -B clean verify           # what CI runs (Surefire + Jacoco)
mvn test                      # tests only; report in target/site/jacoco/index.html
mvn -Dtest=SchedulerServiceTest test           # single class
mvn -Dtest=SchedulerServiceTest#methodName test # single method
mvn package -DskipTests       # produces target/*.jar (consumed by start-local.ps1)
```

### Frontend (from `frontend/`)
```
npm install
npm run dev                   # Vite on :5173, proxies /api → :8080
npm run build                 # outputs dist/, embeds VERSION via define
npm run test                  # vitest single-run (what CI runs)
npm run test:watch
npm run test:coverage         # coverage/index.html
npx vitest run src/test/Nav.test.jsx   # single file
```

### Local full-stack (Windows)
- `start-local.ps1` — kills running `java.exe`, reads `.env`, maps env vars to `-D` system properties, launches the freshly built jar, polls `/health` for UP. Backend log goes to `backend\app.log`; errors to `backend\app-err.log`.
- `START.bat` / `start-backend.bat` / `start-frontend.bat` — simpler convenience launchers.

### Smoke + perf
```
pwsh ./scripts/smoke.ps1 -BaseUrl http://localhost:8080
k6 run -e BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 ./perf/k6-smoke.js
```

### Docker / image
```
docker-compose up -d                          # local stack (app on :8080)
./scripts/build-image.ps1                     # branch-aware tags (master→VERSION+latest, develop→develop-<sha>+develop, release/*→VERSION-rc+staging)
./scripts/build-image.ps1 -Registry ghcr.io/your-org -Push
```

## Architecture — what you need to know across files

### Single-process deployment, multi-replica safe
Frontend ships **inside** the backend jar (`spring.web.resources.static-locations=file:./frontend/dist/,classpath:/static/`). In Kubernetes the same image runs N replicas, so anything stateful must survive pod death and not double-fire:

- **Sessions (fixed 2026-10-09)**: one switch, `site.monitor.session.store` (env `SPRING_SESSION_STORE_TYPE`): `jdbc` in the `prod` profile → Spring Session JDBC (`spring_session` / `spring_session_attributes`, cookie `SESSION`); `memory` (default, local dev + tests; blank/`none` too) → Tomcat session, `JSESSIONID`. `config/SessionStoreAutoConfigurationFilter` (an `AutoConfigurationImportFilter` in `META-INF/spring.factories`) drops Boot's `org.springframework.boot.session.*` auto-configs unless the switch is `jdbc` — Boot 4 moved them into `spring-boot-session-jdbc` and has no on/off property, and `spring.session.store-type` no longer exists (that is why prod silently ran in-memory from the 3.3.6 → 4.1.0 upgrade on 2026-06-17 until this fix). `service/session/SessionStoreService` reports the REAL store (bean present?) to System Health (`session_store`) and does the startup marker cleanup: memory clears every non-TERMINATED `active_session_id` (unchanged), jdbc clears only markers with no live `spring_session` row. Gates: `SessionStoreRestartIntegrationTest` (login → restart on the same DB → same cookie still 200), `SessionStoreServiceJpaTest`, `SessionStoreAutoConfigurationFilterTest`.
- **Scheduler distributed lock**: `SchedulerService` self-manages a `scheduler_lock` table (insert/expire by `locked_until`). Only one pod runs the hourly cert sweep. Lock TTL = `SCHEDULER_LOCK_TTL_MINUTES` (10). Admin can force-release via `DELETE /api/admin/system/scheduler-lock`.
- **Cluster schedule + sweep leader (2026-10-09)**: `service/schedule/ClusterScheduleService` keeps per-monitor due times (`monitor_check_schedule`, key `<type>:<id>`, claim = CAS UPDATE on `next_due_at`), round claims (`round:<name>`, never released, so uptime/daily/cron rounds run once per cluster) and the sweep leader lease (`lease:sweep-leader`, `site.monitor.scheduler.sweep-leader-ttl-ms` 180 s). Every scheduled sweep that feeds `MonitoringOutageService` starts with `if (!isSweepLeader()) return;` because recovery counters and sibling-DOWN records are per-pod memory — a new monitoring sweep must add the same gate. Graceful shutdown releases the lease; DB failure falls back to local runs. Manual "Check now" never goes through the schedule. **Lease renewal is independent of the sweep threads (2026-10-09):** `startSweepLeaderRenewer` (at boot, only with the cluster store) renews the lease on its own daemon thread every `ttl/3` (`renewSweepLeadership`), so 54 `@Scheduled` jobs filling the 8-thread pool for longer than the TTL no longer hand leadership over; a follower's renewer takes an expired lease. It also installs `MonitoringOutageService.setScheduledResultsFence(this::isSweepLeader)`: SCHEDULED results (and `handleDnsSweep`) from a pod that lost the lease are dropped (manual checks pass; a failing fence is open). Shutdown stops the renewer before releasing the lease. Single pod: identical behaviour. Gates: `SchedulerClusterScheduleTest` (renewer/fence cases), `MonitoringOutageServiceTest.scheduledResultsFence_*`.
- **JPA**: `spring.jpa.open-in-view=false` — entities must be DTO-mapped inside the service transaction; lazy associations after return will throw. Hibernate `ddl-auto=update` is the schema-evolution mechanism (no Flyway/Liquibase).

### Auth — custom interceptor, not Spring Security filter chain
`AuthInterceptor` (registered in `WebConfig`) guards `/api/**`. Login goes through `AuthController`, sessions are HttpSession-based with a remember-me cookie validated by `RememberMeService` (token table: `remember_me_tokens`). Three orthogonal lock states on `AppUser`:
- `lockoutUntil` — temporary progressive lockout (durations: 30s→120s→600s→1800s by default). **Since 2026-09-26 there is no automatic permanent lock** (product decision: an anonymous attacker could permanently lock any account): beyond the last level every further violation repeats the last duration (30 min). Attempts made while locked (`LOGIN_FAILED` with outcome `BLOCKED`) don't count, and the counter uses the canonical username.
- **Unknown usernames (2026-10-09):** a non-existent name climbs the SAME ladder as an active account (`service/lockout/LockoutLadder` is the single source for failures-needed and durations), stored in `login_unknown_lockouts` (all pods agree), so status, body and `wait_seconds` are identical at every level; no `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` audit and no AppUser row for it. A failed local login always burns one BCrypt. Gate: `AuthControllerUnknownLockoutParityTest`.
- `permanentLock` — legacy state from before 2026-09-26 (and any admin-set lock); admin-only release. Nothing sets it automatically any more.
- `mustChangePassword` — while set, only `/api/me`, `/api/me/change-password`, `/api/login`, `/api/logout` are reachable. Don't add bypass paths.
- `active=false` (passive, user decision 2026-10-02) — a passive account can NEVER sign in, by any path. Local password, LDAP/AD bind (checked on the EXISTING row before `provisionFromAd`, which never runs, so the user is not reactivated) and remember-me all return `ACCOUNT_INACTIVE` (login 403, otherwise 401; JSON `code`/`error_code`). The "passive" message is shown only after the credentials are verified; a wrong password keeps the generic 401 (no enumeration). Audit `LOGIN_FAILED` outcome BLOCKED does not feed the lockout. `AuthInterceptor` runs the passive check BEFORE `isSessionSuperseded` (5 s per-user cache, `site.monitor.session.inactive-cache-ms`). On a hit it strips the session and leaves a TOMBSTONE (`accountInactive` attribute) instead of `invalidate()`, so every tab, every in-flight request and every pod (JDBC store) sees the same signal — don't "fix" this into `invalidate()`. It also deletes the user's remember-me tokens and audits `SESSION_ENDED_INACTIVE`. Deactivation (`UserService.updateUser`, single + bulk) writes the `TERMINATED:` sentinel and deletes the tokens at once. The client (`utils/accountInactive.js`, `AccountInactiveDialog`) shows a non-dismissable 10 s countdown, then goes to `/?session=inactive`. No passive check per endpoint — the central gate is the design. Notifications: `InactiveRecipientGuard` (60 s snapshot, `site.monitor.notify.inactive-cache-ms`) drops contacts linked to passive users (email + webhook) on every contact path. `EmailNotificationService.doSend` drops passive-only addresses; if none remain it logs `SKIPPED: pasif kullanıcı`. Team member lists show passive members last, with a destructive "Pasif" badge; pickers stay active-only.
- **Code login (OTP) + login methods (2026-10-02/03, user request).** `service/otp/*` handles 6-digit single-use codes sent by push or e-mail (default 45 s each, 30–300 s, configurable per channel).
  - **Storage:** the DB holds only `HMAC(SITE_MONITOR_SECRET_KEY sub-key, challengeId:code)` in `login_otp_challenges`. State changes are conditional UPDATEs: 3 attempts by default, single use.
  - **Limits:** counted in a 15-minute window from the DB. The IP limit returns 429. The per-user request limit, the resend cooldown and the failed-verification suspension are SILENT.
  - **No enumeration:** the request endpoint returns the SAME 200 body for eligible, ineligible and unknown users. An ineligible request creates a DECOY row (`user_id=null`, `SUPPRESSED_*`) whose verification behaves exactly like a wrong code. Ineligible means passive, locked, global admin while `allow-global-admins` is off (default), `mustChangePassword` (2026-10-03: use the temporary password), or no target. Delivery is async (`LoginOtpDeliveryService`). Never add a short-circuit (`LoginOtpServiceFlowTest`).
  - **Contact verification (2026-10-03, user request):**
    - **Switches:** `site.monitor.login.otp.push.require-phone` / `.email.require-email`, default ON, GLOBAL_ONLY.
    - **Request:** while a switch is on, the request also carries `phone` / `email`. The code is sent only if the value matches the username's account.
    - **Matching (`OtpContactMatcher`):**
      - phone: digits only; drop `0090`, `90` (when ≥ 10 digits remain) and the trunk `0`; ≥ 10 digits; compare the last 10; constant-time. `AppUser.phone` = AD `mobile`.
      - e-mail: trim + case-insensitive.
    - **Same answer every time:** a mismatch, no usable registered value, or an unknown user returns the SAME 200 plus a decoy (`SUPPRESSED_CONTACT_MISMATCH` / `NO_PHONE` / `NO_TARGET` / `UNKNOWN_USER`).
    - **Blank but required:** 400 `PHONE_REQUIRED` / `EMAIL_REQUIRED` + `field`. Writes no row and uses no IP quota.
    - **Anti-guessing:** `max-contact-mismatches` (1–20, default 5) per username per 15 min → silent `CONTACT_LOCK`. Requests made while locked are not counted. Password login is unaffected.
    - **Timing:** the mismatch count query runs on EVERY request while the switch is on — no short-circuit. Switch OFF ≡ the old behaviour (no query, no `contact` audit key).
    - **Privacy:** the ENTERED value is never stored, logged or audited; audit gets only `contact`=MATCHED/MISMATCH/MISSING/LOCKED/NOT_CHECKED.
    - **Admin view:** adds `coverage` {active_users, with_phone, with_email} from one aggregate query. The UI warns below 80 %.
    - **Gates:** `LoginOtpContactVerificationTest`, `OtpContactMatcherTest`, `LoginOtpCodeNeverLoggedTest.enteredContactNeverLeaks`.
  - **Login statistics + editable OTP push text (2026-10-03, user request):**
    - **Audit `method`:** every LOGIN, and every password-path LOGIN_FAILED, carries detail `method` (LOCAL / LDAP / REMEMBER_ME / OTP_PUSH / OTP_EMAIL; the legacy value `PASSWORD` means LOCAL). Event types, outcomes, failure_reason and lockout counting are unchanged.
    - **Classification:** single source is `service/loginstats/LoginEventClassifier`. An unknown actor gets no channel. Method-less legacy rows are classified by the account source and marked `estimated`.
    - **Service:** `LoginStatsService` runs one row query (cap 250k → `truncated`) plus one grouped count for the previous period. Results are cached 30 s per period; `fresh` recomputes at most every 5 s.
    - **Endpoints:** `/api/admin/login-methods/stats[/users[/{username}]]` are global-admin only. The user detail goes through IdentityMask.
    - **UI:** tab `lm_tab=stats`, URL prefix `lm_`. The user directory shows channel badges.
    - **Push text keys:** `site.monitor.login.otp.push.title|message-tr|en`, GLOBAL_ONLY, rules in `OtpPushTemplate`:
      - message: `{kod}` exactly once, plus optional `{sure}` / `{saat}`;
      - title: ≤ 60 characters, no placeholders;
      - worst-case length after `PushText.pushSafe` must fit the push limit;
      - an invalid stored template falls back to the default at delivery.
    - **Push test:** `push-test` sends only to the session admin, 3/min, audit `LOGIN_METHODS_PUSH_TEST`.
    - **Client mirror:** `utils/pushSafeText.js` mirrors `PushText` (gate `pushSafeText.test.js`).
    - **Push charset:** `UserPushService.sendDirect` runs `PushText.pushSafe` like the alarm path. The channel is ISO-8859-9, so an unfiltered "—" showed as "?" after the code (`LoginOtpPushSafeTextTest`).
  - **Session:** a successful verification goes through `AuthController.establishSession`, the SAME path as password login (409/forceLogin, remember-me, maintenance, passive, lock, LOGIN method=OTP_*).
  - **The code is never written anywhere:** not to logs, DB columns, `notification_logs`, push delivery rows or audit (`LoginOtpCodeNeverLoggedTest`). `RequestLoggingFilter` skips `/api/login/otp/` bodies. Push goes straight to the gateway (identifier = username, no outbox row). E-mail goes through the funnel with `force=true`.
  - **Lockout:** OTP failures are not `LOGIN_FAILED` and never feed the password lockout.
  - **LDAP toggle:** with `site.monitor.login.ldap-enabled=false`, every non-local name and a wrong local password get the same 401 `LDAP_LOGIN_DISABLED`. The bootstrap `admin` always reaches local verification.
  - **Settings:** keys live in group `login-methods`, GLOBAL_ONLY, at Settings → Güvenlik → Giriş Yöntemleri. Endpoints: `GET /api/public/login-methods` and `/api/login/otp/{request,verify}` (PUBLIC).
  - **Adding a channel or event:** update `AuditEventCatalog` + `audit.ev.*` + `auditFormat` SPECIAL.
- **System-wide bulk deactivation (2026-10-02)** — `UserBulkDeactivationController` (`/api/admin/users/bulk-deactivate[/preview|/{opId}/undo]`, `/bulk-operations`), GLOBAL admin only. Criteria are AND-combined (scope all/teams, N days without login incl. never-logged-in accounts created ≥ N days ago, source, role). Server-side it ALWAYS excludes every `systemRole=ADMIN` account (global + scoped), the operator and already-passive users. Apply recomputes the set; a mismatch with `expected_count` returns 409 `BULK_LIST_CHANGED`. It is capped at 5000 and runs in chunks of 200, each user through `UserService.updateUser(active=false)` so the passive gate kicks them. The operation is stored in `user_bulk_operations` (BOUNDED, never deleted). Undo reactivates only members still carrying THIS op's TERMINATED marker and not in a later un-undone op; the claim is atomic, so a second undo gets 409. Weekly-report approval links are refused (`WEEKLY_REPORT_LINK_DENIED`) only when EVERY addressee of the latest `SUBMIT_PO` mail is passive; otherwise the check fails open.
- **System maintenance mode (2026-10-02, user decision)** — Settings → Platform → Sistem Bakımı, GLOBAL admin only (backend 403 for a scoped admin).
  - **Phase:** `system_maintenance_windows`. The phase is DERIVED from time, never stored (`SystemMaintenanceService.phaseOf`: PLANNED → ANNOUNCED (`announce_hours`) → WARNING (`warn_minutes`) → ACTIVE → ENDED / CANCELLED). It is cached per pod for ≤ 5 s (`site.monitor.system-maintenance.cache-ms`) and fails open.
  - **Who gets in:** while ACTIVE only GLOBAL admins (ADMIN with no `viewTeamIds`) may log in or stay. `AuthInterceptor` order is passive → **maintenance** → `isSessionSuperseded`. The cut reuses the passive TOMBSTONE pattern (attribute `systemMaintenance`, remember-me tokens deleted, `activeSessionId` cleared, audit `SESSION_ENDED_MAINTENANCE`) and returns 401 `MAINTENANCE`.
  - **Login:** local and LDAP check maintenance after the credential check and before provisioning, and return 403 `MAINTENANCE`. The audit is `LOGIN_FAILED` BLOCKED, which does not count toward the IP limit or failed-login stamps.
  - **Client:** `/api/session/ping` and `/api/me` carry an additive `maintenance` block plus `server_now`. The client computes phases on SERVER time, and the per-second tick lives only in `SystemMaintenanceLayer`, never in App. A 401 `MAINTENANCE` never redirects: it opens the non-dismissable countdown, then `/?session=maintenance`.
  - **Public status:** `GET /api/public/system-maintenance` (no-store, no ids/counters). Its path must not contain "status" (`StatusPageControllerTest.notPublic`).
  - **Mute toggle:** "Bildirimler bakım boyunca sussun" defaults OFF, and OFF ≡ byte-identical behaviour. When ON, mail/webhook/push/NOC are suppressed for every trigger except MANUAL resend. The trace is `notification_logs` trigger `SYSTEM_MAINTENANCE` + `SKIPPED: sistem bakımı`, push `SKIPPED_SYSTEM_MAINTENANCE`, plus a `system_maintenance_suppressions` row. No storms form while muted. Timed escalation steps and the quiet digest are held until catch-up.
  - **Catch-up:** `SystemMaintenanceJobService` runs under `scheduler_lock` key `system-maintenance`. It sets `last_re_alert_at=NULL` on open, unacked alarms whose INITIAL/ESCALATION was suppressed, so the next sweep sends one INITIAL. Alarms closed during maintenance get nothing.
  - **Counters and mails:** counters and job stamps are `updatable=false` and written by native claims only (never via an entity save). Announcement and correction mails are opt-in, sent BCC in chunks of 100, and exclude passive users. Weekly-report approve links show a maintenance page.
  - **Gates:** `AuthInterceptorMaintenanceTest`, `AuthControllerMaintenanceTest`, `SystemMaintenanceNotificationTest`, `SystemMaintenanceJobServiceTest`, `SystemMaintenanceQueriesTest`, `AppSystemMaintenance.test.jsx`.
  - **Ended notice + end mail (2026-10-02, user request):**
    - **State:** for `site.monitor.system-maintenance.ended-notice-minutes` (60; 0 = off) after a NON-cancelled window ends, `clientBlock` and `publicStatus` return an additive `state:"ended"`. The public variant carries no id or revision. An announced, warning or active window always wins. `live()` looks back over that period in its single cached query.
    - **UI:** a dismissable success strip (`maint-ended`, dismiss kind `ended` per id + revision; global admins see it too), the login card with `data-state="completed"`, and a status-page note.
    - **End mail:** controlled per window by `email_on_end` (default TRUE, null = TRUE). `SystemMaintenanceJobService` sends `Kind.ENDED` exactly once (`claimEndMail` on `end_mail_at`; `end_mail_status` and `end_mail_count` are `updatable=false`; audit `SYSTEM_MAINTENANCE_MAIL` kind ENDED) to the same recipients as the announcement, in BCC chunks of 100.
    - **Immediate windows** never send announce or correction mails; their recipients get only the end mail.
    - **Subject:** written `[Site Monitor] …`, because `EmailResponsiveContractTest` rejects the one-word brand in mails.

Spring Security is **not** on the classpath as a filter chain — only `spring-security-crypto` for BCrypt. Don't pull in `spring-boot-starter-security`; it conflicts with the manual interceptor.

### Single active session per user
A user holds at most one live session. `UserService` keeps `AppUser.activeSessionId` (newest login wins) plus `lastSeenAt`; the SPA pings `/api/session/ping` (~15s) to keep it fresh. On every `/api/**` request `AuthInterceptor.isSessionSuperseded(user, currentSessionId)` compares the registered id — if it differs, the interceptor invalidates the session, clears the remember-me cookie, and returns **401** (the client then redirects to `/?session=expired`). Login returns **409** when another live session exists; the frontend re-posts with `forceLogin=true` after a confirmation modal. Admin force-logout (`POST /api/admin/system/terminate-session`) sets `activeSessionId` to a `TERMINATED:<uuid>` sentinel that can never match a real session. Two traps: sessions persist in the Spring Session JDBC store but this registry is a single column, so stale ids are cleared at startup (`SessionStoreService.clearStaleActiveSessionMarkers`: jdbc clears only ids with no live `spring_session` row, memory clears all; TERMINATED sentinels always survive); and `activeSessionId = null` means *grandfathered*, not *kicked* — `isSessionSuperseded` returns false for null.

### Opaque user ids (2026-10-08, user decision: "bir kullanıcı başka bir kullanıcının id'sini okuyamasın")
- **Rule:** a GLOBAL admin sees and may send numeric `app_users.id` (byte-identical to before). Everyone else (AUDIT and scoped admins included) sees the opaque `app_users.public_id` (UUID, assigned once, never changes) and may send only that; a numeric user id from them resolves to "not found".
- **Output — one gate:** `config/UserRefResponseAdvice` converts every Jackson response for a non-global viewer into a fresh tree (`UserRefWire`). Values under user-ref KEYS (`user_id`, `manager_id`, `leader_id`, `actor_id`, `author_id`, `editing_user_id`, `contacted_user_id`, … plus camelCase, incl. inside the JSON-text fields `changes`/`detail`/`details`/`snapshot`, and `resource_id` when `resource_type=USER`) become the UUID; an unknown user becomes null. An ambiguous `id` key is NOT rewritten by name: `AppUser.id` carries `@JsonSerialize(UserRef.LongSerializer)` and map rows use `UserRef.of(id)` (user directory, team members, bulk result rows). Outside the gate (storage, logs, global admin) both write a plain number.
- **Input:** every endpoint that takes a user id resolves it with `UserPublicIds.resolve(svc, raw, session)` (path ids are `String`). Controllers get the service as an optional field (`@Autowired(required=false)`) so slice/unit tests without the bean keep the old numeric parsing. Hand-written outputs bypass the gate: the audit CSV/JSON export converts itself (`ExportView`).
- **Adding a field/endpoint:** name a user-id field so its snake_case is in `UserRefWire.KEYS` (or `@JsonIgnore` it), and use `UserRef.of` for a user under an `id` key. The frontend never `Number()`s a user id (`utils/userRef.js`: `userRefValue`, `sameUser`).
- **Gates:** `UserRefCoverageGateTest` (model fields), `UserRefWireTest`, `UserPublicIdsTest`, `UserRefResponseAdviceTest`, `opaqueUserIds.test.jsx`.

### Permissions
Per-resource permission model layered on top of `systemRole` (`USER` / `AUDIT` / `ADMIN`) and org role (`PO`/`TECH`/`MANAGER`/`CLEVEL`). Single source of truth: `PermissionCatalog.ALL` — every resource key + allowed actions (`view`/`edit`/`execute`). The Permission Matrix UI renders from this; bootstrap seed is driven from it. **If you add a new resource_key in code, add it to `PermissionCatalog` in the same change** — otherwise the matrix UI won't show it and grants won't bootstrap.

**7/24 monitoring team (2026-10-04, user decision).**
- **Setting:** Settings → 7/24 → "7/24 izleme ekibi takımları" (`noc_settings.operator_team_ids`). `PUT /api/admin/noc/operator-teams` is GLOBAL admin only (audit `NOC_TEAMS_UPDATE`); scoped admin / AUDIT see it read-only.
- **Who:** active members of those teams are 7/24 operators WITHOUT a role change. `NocOperatorService` keeps a 30 s snapshot; `AuthInterceptor.syncNocOperator` writes the session attributes `nocOperator` / `nocTeamIds` on every request, so revocation needs no re-login.
- **Reads:** operators read all monitoring (alarms, incidents, maintenance, status, storms, the nine monitor types, inventory/certs, overview, notes, search) through `SessionScope.canViewMonitoring` / `seesAllMonitoring` / `monitoringViewTeamIds`, plus the dynamic grants in `PermissionCatalog.NOC_OPERATOR_GRANTS` (since 2026-10-13 also `oncall.read` and `data_quality.view`, user request: "7/24 ekibi kişisel panoda tüm sistemleri görsün" — those two endpoints already read org-wide for operators; the grant keeps the widgets even if the matrix turns them off for USER; `system_health.read` stays excluded).
- **Live scope for the UI (2026-10-13):** `GET /api/me/permissions` carries an additive `scope` = `{monitoring_all (seesAllMonitoring), noc_operator, noc_console (= `NocCallLogService.seesAllAlerts`, the console's own gate — NOT the matrix snapshot: a scoped admin gets `noc_calls.write` from the ADMIN row but the console refuses them; fallback without the service: global viewer ∨ operator)}`, refreshed with the permission snapshot every 60 s (`usePermissions().scope`, null on an old server) — joining / leaving a 7/24 team shows without a re-login. Kişisel Panom uses it for the console widget's visibility and shows a scope chip ("Tüm kurum (7/24 ekibi)" / "Tüm kurum" / "Takımlarınız"). Gates: `AuthControllerTest.myPermissions_carriesScope`, `PermissionCatalogTest.nocOperatorGrantsArePinned`, `PermissionsProvider.test.jsx`, `MyDashNocWidgets.test.jsx`.
- **Writes:** operators gain only `noc_calls.write` and comments on any alarm they can read.
- **Never widen writes or admin:** `viewTeamIds` is untouched. `canManage`, `canOperateTeam`, `canWriteInventory`, ack / resolve, incident write, the audit log and admin areas NEVER read the flag. A new READ path uses the monitoring helpers; a WRITE path never does. Memo keys use `seesAllMonitoring`.
- **Console:** `GET /api/noc/console` (memoised 15 s per window, batch-loaded, max 1000 rows). `GET /api/noc/console/alerts/{id}/call-sheet` is the ONLY endpoint that exposes call-list phone numbers (operators / writers).
- **Alert history:** `noc=sent` = an OPEN `noc_deliveries` row with SENT* / QUEUED_RETRY*.
- **Legacy:** AUDIT + `noc_calls.write` operators keep working.
- **Gates:** `NocOperatorServiceTest`, `SessionScopeTest`, `InventoryOrgVisibilityAdminTest` (operator variants), `AuditControllerTest` / `SystemControllerTest` noc cases, `AuthInterceptorNocOperatorTest`, `NocConsole.test.jsx`.

**Team access sharing (2026-10-12, user request: "A takımı her neyi görüyor, siliyor, editliyor ise yetki verdiği kişi de aynı o ekipteki gibi").**
- **Data:** `team_access_grants` (`TeamAccessGrant`, `@DynamicUpdate`, UNIQUE owner_team_id × `grantee_key` "T:<team>" | "U:<user>"; `access_level` VIEW < EXECUTE < EDIT < DELETE (`service/access/TeamAccessLevel`, cumulative); `expires_at` UTC = end of the chosen Istanbul day; note ≤ 500; ≤ 200 per owner team). A TEAM grant applies to the grantee team's ACTIVE members (primary or extra membership); a USER grant to that active user. Passive / deleted owner team, passive grantee team or user and expired grants never apply. Team delete / user delete remove their grants.
- **Session, not role:** `TeamAccessGrantService` keeps a 30 s snapshot (`site.monitor.team-access.cache-ms`, read failure keeps the previous one); `AuthInterceptor.syncTeamGrants` (after the NOC sync, every request) writes the cumulative lists `grantViewTeamIds ⊇ grantExecTeamIds ⊇ grantEditTeamIds ⊇ grantDeleteTeamIds` only when they change. Revocation needs no re-login (≤ 30 s, other pods too). `viewTeamIds` / `manageTeamIds` / `systemRole` are never touched.
- **What each level opens (SessionScope helpers — use them, never re-derive):** VIEW → every monitoring READ of the team (`canViewWithGrants` / `viewTeamIdsWithGrants`, and through them `canViewMonitoring` / `monitoringViewTeamIds`): certificates (+ card extras, shared-cert window), inventory, the nine monitor types, alarms, incidents (read, incl. those the owner team created), notes, storms, status page, overview, activity, TLS grade, weak-algorithm / crypto inventory, hygiene, manual certs, maintenance windows (read), k6 team templates, channel technical error reports (release 9: channels, reports, PDF, history, template read-only). EXECUTE → also "Check now", diagnose, TLS rescan, health refresh, live registration lookup (`canExecuteTeam`), alarm ack / resolve / comments. EDIT → also edit monitors and inventory, group rename, renewal confirmation (`canOperateTeam`, `canWriteInventory`, `inventoryWriteTest`, `grantedEdit`), and the CONTENT of channel technical error reports (open a report, values, images, apply template — never send / approve / channel / template). DELETE → also delete / restore, old manual-cert versions, bulk inventory delete (`canDeleteTeamResource`).
- **Never shared:** moving a record to another team (`resolveTeamChange` needs `canOperateTeamOwn` on BOTH teams — an EDIT grantee can neither pull the owner's monitor into their team nor push their own into the owner team; a refusal caused by a grant is a 403 with a message, never a silent "saved"; users with no grants keep the old silent rule byte-identically); re-notify, the recipient preview and bulk re-notify (`requireAlertNotifyScope`; rows carry `notify_scope:false`); people lists — on-call rotation / current on-call and the 7/24 member + call list (`canViewMonitoringOwn`), NOC call records, and on an alarm read ONLY through a grant (`AdminController.alertReadOnlyViaGrant`) the detail's `oncall` is empty and `/notifications`, `/push-deliveries`, `/storm-push` hide recipient names / addresses / push usernames (address COUNT only), notification groups (the monitor form's group picker shows "saved group — can't be read here" locked, value kept), escalation contacts; team management (`canManage`), notifications (a grantee is never a recipient), incident WRITE (`inOwnIncidentScope`; rows read only through a grant / 7/24 carry `can_edit:false`), certificate notes write, audit log, admin areas; channel technical error reports: channel create / edit / delete, template save, executive recipients, approval request, SEND, report delete, the Settings overview — and their people data (executive addresses, send recipients, approver names) (release 9). A new WRITE path picks the matching helper; a new READ path uses `canViewWithGrants` / `viewTeamIdsWithGrants` (memo keys too); a people/management read uses `canViewMonitoringOwn` / `canView`.
- **Row flags:** monitor list rows `can_check` / `can_edit` / `can_delete` (the "Check now" trigger response has no flags — `utils/monitorDetailSync.withRowFlags` keeps them on the open detail; card actions render for check-only rows with no Edit/Duplicate). Rows readable ONLY through a grant carry `shared_access:true` + `can_check` (inventory list / by-domain, `/certificates/list`, uptime overview; absent for everyone else) — the UI does not treat them as "another team's read-only record" (Alerts / Changes tabs stay) but takes actions from the flags. Alarm rows read only through a grant carry `act_block:"shared_view"` ("view only" note instead of the standalone-owner note). Dashboard cards (cached `/certificates`, no per-viewer flags) use `/me` `access_grants [{team_id, team_name, level}]` — ONLY teams the user cannot already `canView` (a manager's own / managed team never appears there) — via `rowGrantAccess` (App `certGrantAccess`, `checkableCerts`; CertificateModal `grantAccess`). EDIT / DELETE teams are added to the add-form team pickers (`grantWriteTeamsFromMe` → App `writeTeams`; for admin roles merged into the `/admin/teams` list by `mergeWriteTeams` — same array back when nothing is added — in the monitor pages, InventoryManager and ManualCertsPage), never to navigation or management; the inventory form offers no team move on a grant-only row.
- **API:** `GET /api/admin/team-access[?team]` (`teams.sharing/view`; global viewer all, otherwise teams the viewer manages; rows carry `can_edit`, `owner_active`, `grantee_active`), `POST` / `PUT {id}` (only the fields sent: level, `expires_on`, note) / `DELETE {id}` (`teams.sharing/edit` + `canManage(owner)`; a row the caller could not list → 404, listed but not manageable (AUDIT) → 403; 409 `GRANT_EXISTS` / `GRANT_LIMIT`; field errors `VALIDATION_FAILED` + `field`), `GET /api/me/shared-with-me`. Audit `TEAM_ACCESS_GRANT_CREATE|UPDATE|DELETE`. `grantee_user_id` is an opaque user ref.
- **UI:** Admin → Organisation → "Erişim paylaşımı" (`g_tab=sharing`, `components/admin/access/*`): shared-with-me card, KPI tiles (counted after search / team / level filters; type and state tiles exclude each other; "Total" clears only them), search + owner / level filters (`g_aq`, `g_team`, `g_atype`, `g_alevel`, `g_astate`), table ≥ 860 px container else cards, labelled actions menu (edit / remove with confirm), `TeamAccessGrantDialog` (level choice cards, DELETE level asks again, edit sends only changed fields, server field errors under the field incl. level). A 403 on the list (no `teams.sharing/view`) is the "you manage no team" empty state, not an error. Team row menu "Erişimi paylaş" jumps there with `g_team`. Pickers use `SearchableSelect wrapLabels` so long AD names wrap; level / state badges are `rounded-md overflow-visible`.
- **Gates:** `TeamAccessGrantServiceTest`, `SessionScopeGrantTest`, `TeamAccessControllerTest`, `AuthInterceptorTeamGrantTest`, `AlertStandaloneOwnerScopeTest.teamAccessGrant*`, `MonitoringControllerTest.teamAccessGrant_*`, `CertificateControllerTest.healthConfirm_grantNeedsEdit`, `IncidentControllerTest.sharedIncidentIsReadOnly`, `teamAccessModel.test.js` (reads the Java level order; `rowGrantAccess`, `withRowFlags`), `TeamAccessManager.test.jsx`, `MonitorCardActions.test.jsx`, `e2e/team-access.spec.js` (390/768/1280).

The inventory form keeps group / 7/24 (`AdminController.requireSameInventoryRouting`). UI: rows carry `shared_access:true` → `NotifyChannels locked`, `NocNotifyField disabled`, team picker disabled (`formTeamSelect`; own rows drop grant-only teams from the picker); `InventoryFormModal` locks team / group / 7/24 itself when `record.shared_access`. `NotificationGroupSelect` locks only on a 403/404 answer; a network / 5xx failure shows "couldn't be loaded", stays editable.

The risk is stated where the access is given: `TeamAccessGrantDialog` shows `access.k6Warn` for EDIT / DELETE (scripts run with the team's encrypted env values). Live check: a grantee USER at VIEW / EXECUTE / EDIT / DELETE across all nine types — API + UI "Düzenle → Kaydet" / "Sil" (all 200 at their level, 403 below). Review rules (same day): the k6 diagnose response (`url`, `candidates`, its 400 messages, leg `error` / `output_tail`) masks the monitor's SECRET env values (`ScriptedCheckerService.secretValues` + `SecretMask.maskValues`); a masked `url` sent back (the dialog pre-fills it) is mapped to the raw candidate before probing; `checks_json` is masked at run time AND at read time (list `maskScriptedSecrets`, check-history page — rows written before the change) — the run output already was; `restoreChange` on a k6 row applies the update rules (name unique in the team → 400, open alarms follow a renamed monitor — the alarm key is the NAME — a restored pause closes them, and a restore that reactivates clears `disabledReason` / `disabledAt` like the update); on a locked form a loaded draft never brings back routing fields or the team, the lock survives a create whose form stays open (`routing_locked` + `can_delete: false` put on `modal`, then the list row's flags via `freshestRow` after the reload — the role fallback counted a grant team as "own" and drew a 403 Delete), and a saved script of ANOTHER monitor resets its secret rows to `value_set: false`. Owners with live, manageable EDIT / DELETE grants see a notice on the sharing page until they dismiss it (`k6EditGrants` — every such grant, no date cut: grants made on the old release before the deploy saw the old text, and the server's zone-less UTC timestamps parse as local time; dismissal per user in `sm.access.k6Notice`). Gates: `MonitoringControllerTest.scripted_editGrant_editsFully_routingStillGuarded|scripted_list_grantFlagsMatchOtherTypes|createScripted_inGrantTeam_routingStaysDefault|scriptedDiagnose_masksSecretValuesInUrlAndLegs|scriptedDiagnose_maskedUrlRoundTripsToRawCandidate|scriptedDiagnose_invalidUrlMessageIsMasked|scripted_list_masksSecretsInStoredChecksJson|restore_scripted_byGrantee_restores|restore_scripted_duplicateName_isRejected|restore_scripted_reactivation_clearsAnomalyReason`, `ScriptedSecretValuesTest`, `ScriptedMonitorPage.grant.test.jsx`, `teamAccessModel.test.js`, `TeamAccessManager.test.jsx` (k6 warning + notice).
  - **Notes:** a grant-only viewer needs an operable owner team to write a note; the note is stamped with the owner team (`MonitorNotesController`). DNS change-history restore needs the DELETE gate (`canRestore`).
  - **Bulk selection** never includes `shared_access` rows (inventory `canSelectRow`, certificate table `bulkSelectable`) — bulk activate / deactivate / contacts / tier are team management and the server skips them; single-row actions stay.
  - **Fresh grant levels:** `GET /api/me/access-grants` (same shape and filter as `/me`) is part of the dashboard load wave (`grants` source) — a new / removed share updates card actions without re-login.
  - **People never shown through a grant:** NOC call records are masked on grant-only alarms (`maskGrantOnlyNocCalls`); notification rows show only a status class (`statusClass`), no message / sender / push response.
  - **Limits:** expiry ≤ 2099-12-31; the per-owner cap counts LIVE (non-expired) grants (`countLiveByOwnerTeamId`); a unique-constraint race → `GRANT_EXISTS`; a no-op PUT saves and audits nothing. The snapshot cache uses a generation counter: a build that raced a write is not stored; `invalidate()` marks the snapshot stale instead of dropping it (a failed rebuild keeps the previous view).
  - **DELETE level** lets every grantee (for a team grant: all its members, any role) delete — the dialog and hints say so.

The forms reset to `ROUTING_DEFAULTS` / `INVENTORY_ROUTING_DEFAULTS` (`withRoutingDefaults`, same object back when already default) and lock the fields as soon as such a team is picked or duplicated into.

The DNS update resolves a team change only for standalone rows (as before round 5); a derived Port row whose body team equals its effective (inventory) team is an alignment, not a move (round 7).

### The check pipeline
`SchedulerService.@Scheduled(cron=…)` (default `0 0 * * * *`) acquires the distributed lock and fans out to `CertificateCheckerService`, plus parallel sweeps for `PortCheckerService`, `DnsCheckerService`, `UptimeHttpCheckerService`. Concurrency uses a dedicated `certCheckExecutor` `ThreadPoolTaskExecutor` (bean in `WebConfig`, sized by `EXECUTOR_CORE_SIZE` / `EXECUTOR_MAX_SIZE` / `EXECUTOR_QUEUE_CAPACITY`).

- `CertificateCheckerService` retries **only NETWORK-class** failures once (`CHECK_RETRY=true`). SSL/DNS/CERT errors are never retried — they're real findings.
- TLS handshake fingerprint: `TLS_MODE=browser` forces TLS 1.2 + ALPN `[h2,http/1.1]` so WAFs (Akamai/F5/Imperva) don't RST. Switch to `default` only to debug.
- Outbound proxy: `HTTP_PROXY_HOST`/`PORT` + `NO_PROXY` (suffix match) — applies to OCSP/CRL fetches too.
- `ChainValidationService` does chain + OCSP (primary) + CRL (fallback) via BouncyCastle. CRL responses are Caffeine-cached (`CRL_CACHE_TTL_HOURS`).
- **Revocation reason (2026-10-08, user request: "show the address fields even when empty, remove misleading warnings").**
  - **Backend:** `checkRevocationDetailed` returns status + reason (`RevocationReason`: OCSP, CRL, NO_ENDPOINTS, UNSUPPORTED_SCHEME, UNREACHABLE, NO_ISSUER, NOT_CHECKED, PENDING) + attempts JSON. These are stored in `latest_checks` / `certificate_checks` `revocation_reason|revocation_detail`. The status verdict and the network calls are unchanged.
  - **Health row:** NO_ENDPOINTS is NA (not an issue, no "check access" advice). The OCSP/CRL evidence keys are ALWAYS present; null renders "Sertifikada tanımlı değil".
  - **Legacy rows:** with no reason, a read certificate (fingerprint present) with both addresses blank is treated as NO_ENDPOINTS (`RevocationReason.effective` = `utils/revocationInfo.js`).
  - **Adding a reason or attempt code:** add TR+EN `hlth.revReason.*` / `hlth.revFail.*` and keep the JS list in sync (`revocationInfo.test.js` reads the Java constants).
- **Bulk network outage detection**: if `NETWORK_ERROR_THRESHOLD` (default 0.50) of a run fails with network errors and at least `NETWORK_MIN_ERRORS` (3) failed, individual cert alarms for that run are suppressed and a single `NetworkOutageEvent` + admin email is raised instead. This is intentional — don't "fix" it by always alerting.
- **Per-domain proxy override (`use_proxy`)**: `CertificateInventory.useProxy` (column `use_proxy`, default false) flips a single domain to go through `HTTP_PROXY_HOST/PORT`. `SchedulerService.runCheckForDomains` builds a `domain→forceProxy` map and calls `checkAsync(domain, port, forceProxy)`. Decision: `forceProxy && proxyEnabled() && !shouldBypassProxy(domain)`. Direct outbound stays the default — added so production can route a couple of WAF-quirky domains through the corporate proxy without dragging the rest along.

### Inventory metadata that drives behavior
`CertificateInventory` carries a wide operational schema beyond the cert URL. The fields the rest of the system actually branches on:
- **`tier` (1-4)** — 1: Customer-Facing Prod, 2: Internal Prod, 3: UAT/Pre-Prod, 4: Dev/Sandbox. Drives sort order, criticality badges, and escalation contact selection.
- **`useProxy`** — see above.
- **`teamId` / `ugTeamId`** — primary (SY) and secondary (UG = Uygulama Geliştirici) team ownership for escalation routing.
- **`active`** — `false` = paused (monitoring stops, record kept). **Deletion is PERMANENT (2026-10-07, user decision: "silme işlemi her şekilde kalıcı olsun")** — there is no bin, no restore, no `inventory.purge` permission and no auto-purge setting any more. Every inventory delete (single, bulk, network or manual row) goes through `PermanentDeletionService.deleteInventory`: owner-scoped silent alarm close, then in one transaction the derived Port/DNS monitors + their checks, uptime checks, certificate checks, `latest_checks`, notes + revisions, weak-algorithm exception, certificate diagnostic runs, manual versions and the row (flushed, so the name is free at once). Kept on purpose: closed `alert_events`, audit, `monitor_change_log` (DELETE row, "kalıcı silme"), notification/activity logs, `pinned_cas`. Standalone Port/DNS delete is permanent too (alarms closed, checks deleted, no DNS revival); inventory-derived Port/DNS rows still only pause. `deleted_at` columns stay (raw SQL reads them) but nothing sets them. A deleted name never blocks re-adding (create, rename, import, manual key, suggested key, by-domain all ignore old bin rows). The one-time `DeletedRecordsPurge` (marker `2026-10-07-permanent-delete-bin-purge`, audit `SYSTEM_DELETED_RECORDS_PURGE` with the name lists) purged every legacy bin row at startup. UI: deletes remove the card at once and hide it for 10 min via `utils/recentlyDeleted.js` (per-pod Caffeine caches can return the deleted row from another pod); confirmations say permanent and name the records. Never reintroduce a soft delete or a delete without a confirmation.
- Boolean operational toggles: `externalVendor`, `openshift`, `sslPinning`, `internalCert`, `jksKeystore`, `wafEnabled`, `evCertificate`, `actionRequired`, `transferredToSy`, etc. These are admin-facing flags that show up in InventoryManager and on reports — they don't gate the check pipeline.

When you add a new boolean toggle: append to the `INVENTORY_FLAGS` array in `frontend/src/utils/inventoryFlags.js` (the single source — `inventory-flags-sync.test.jsx` locks its order against the Java side), add the EMPTY default and save-payload entry in `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryFormModal.jsx` (the form lives there, not in `InventoryManager.jsx` — it is shared with the dashboard card's Edit/Duplicate buttons), add the diff-builder entry in `AdminController.buildInventoryDiff`, and add the setter call in `updateInventory` — missing the setter is the most common bug (the field "saves" but disappears on reload).

### Self-monitoring / watchdog (2026-10-11, user request)
- `service/watchdog/*`: `WatchdogService.tick` every `site.monitor.watchdog.interval-ms` (60 s) under `scheduler_lock` `watchdog` (via `SchedulerService.callWithSchedulerLock`) → `WatchdogProbes.runAll` (fixed, cheap queries: sweep-leader lease, `MonitoringOverviewService.orgTypes()` stale ratio, `MAX(latest_checks.checked_at)`, notification attempted/sent in 30 min, stuck PENDING push rows + circuit breaker, `DatabaseHealthService`) → `WatchdogTransition` (pure: `confirm-checks` consecutive results to flip, reminders every `reminder-hours`, UNKNOWN never flips, NA (check no longer applies) closes an open problem with a RECOVER, undelivered `*_FAILED` retried every 15 min) → ONE combined `WatchdogNotifier.send` per round (mail `WatchdogMail`, webhook, DIRECT push via `sendDirect` — never the outbox it monitors; system-maintenance mute → `SKIPPED: sistem bakımı`; every send logged in `notification_logs` trigger `WATCHDOG`, alertEventId 0). State in `watchdog_state` (one row per `WatchdogCheck`); DB unreachable → in-memory per pod + database-only local evaluation; when the DB is readable again a NEWER memory state replaces the stored row, so the outage's recovery is notified (2026-10-12). One evaluation per interval cluster-wide: the lock is released after each round, so a scheduled tick SKIPS (`RECENT`) when the stored states were evaluated within the last interval by any pod. A pod that skips with `RECENT` drops memory states not newer than the stored rows (no stale "problem" reused in the next outage). A SKIPPED e-mail (channel off / passive-only) is not an attempt (→ NO_CHANNEL, no 15-min retry loop); system push skips passive accounts and follows `push_lang`. Endpoints: PUBLIC `GET /api/public/health/watchdog` (status + check keys only; 503 on confirmed CRITICAL or evaluation older than max(5 min, 5 × interval)), `GET /api/admin/system/watchdog` (`system_health.read`), `POST …/evaluate` (`system_health.actions/execute`, audit `WATCHDOG_EVALUATE`; runs under the same lock — 409 `WATCHDOG_BUSY` while another round runs, 409 `WATCHDOG_DISABLED` when off and the button is hidden), `POST …/test` (global admin, 3/min, audit `WATCHDOG_TEST`). Settings group `watchdog` (`WatchdogSettings` validates ranges/addresses; recipients / push-users / webhook GLOBAL_ONLY). UI: first System Health section (`health/WatchdogSection`, `watchdogModel.watchdogReasons` feeds the overall band). Adding a check: enum in `WatchdogCheck` (labels + problem text), a probe, a `WatchdogMessages.detail` case. Gates: `WatchdogTransitionTest`, `WatchdogProbesTest`, `WatchdogServiceTest`, `WatchdogNotifierTest`, `WatchdogSettingsTest`, `WatchdogControllerTest`, `WatchdogPostgresTest`, `SystemHealth.test.jsx`, `watchdogModel.test.js`, `e2e/watchdog.spec.js`. Runbook: "Kendi kendini izleme".
- **Watchdog notification states (2026-10-12):** a problem this pod opened and ANNOUNCED from memory during a DB outage gets a RECOVER when the newer stored row (another pod) is healthy (stored status exactly `OK` AND last result `OK` / `NA` — `WatchdogService.storedHealthy`; a status-less row is not a recovery, review rounds 4–5). If the stored row is not healthy YET (status OK with an unconfirmed PROBLEM / UNKNOWN last result) the announced problem waits in the per-pod `pendingRecoveries` map (one entry per check, survives `memory.clear()`): RECOVER once the row is healthy, dropped when the row turns PROBLEM (that row then owns it) or another pod announced the recovery (review round 6, `WatchdogServiceTest.pendingRecoverySurvivesPruning`) — pruning no longer swallows it (`announcedProblem` + `recoverOrphans`). System-maintenance mute stores `ALERT_MUTED` / `REMIND_MUTED`; nothing is retried while muted, and when the mute ends a still-open problem is sent ONCE. No channel → `*_NOCHANNEL` (not retried, reminder timer runs, the UI never says "sent"; keys via `watchdogModel.notifiedKey`). Gates: `WatchdogServiceTest.mutedProblemIsSentAfterMaintenance|staleMemoryPrunedOnRecentSkip|prunedProblemStillOpenIsNotRecovered`.

### System metrics page (2026-10-13, user request: "sistem sağlığı üzerinden site monitör metriklerini detaylı izlemek")
- **Sampler:** `service/metrics/SystemMetricSampler` writes ONE row per pod per minute (cron second 7, no lock — every pod writes only its own `instance_id`) into `system_metric_samples`: process / system CPU, heap / non-heap, threads, Hikari active / idle / pending / max, `certCheckExecutor` active / pool / max / queue / capacity, HTTP = the sum of the minutes COMPLETED since the previous sample (`HttpMetricsService.completedMinutesAfter(lastTs, 10 + 1)` — the minute rotation runs on its own timer, so reading "the last minute" counted a minute twice or skipped one) plus `http_minutes` = the minute SPAN from the last consumed minute to the newest (a quiet minute whose bucket was never created is a 0-request minute, not a missing one; the cap asks for 10 + 1 minutes so a real truncation is told apart from exactly 10), deltas for GC, completed tasks, caller-runs, logback WARN / ERROR and Caffeine hits / misses, and `delta_minutes` = minutes since the previous sample (null on the baseline row). The first sample after a start is the baseline (deltas null) and a counter that went backwards gives null — never a fake 0; an unreadable source stays null. On a clean shutdown (`@PreDestroy`) the pod writes a final row with `shutdown_marker = true` (identity + minute only). Boot 4's `LogbackMetricsAutoConfiguration` does not activate in this app, so the sampler binds Micrometer `LogbackMetrics` once at `ApplicationReadyEvent` ONLY when no `logback.events` meter exists and unbinds it on shutdown. Micrometer registers `logback.events` as a **FunctionCounter** — look it up with `find(…).meter()`, never `.counter()` (that returned null: no counts, and a second filter on top of Boot's). A save failure never breaks the flow (one WARN per hour). Keys: `site.monitor.metrics.system.enabled` (`SYSTEM_METRICS_ENABLED`, true), `site.monitor.metrics.system.retention-days` (7, group `monitoring`, RetentionCatalog `system-metric-samples`).
- **Reads:** `SystemMetricsQueryService` aggregates in SQL — native query over a derived table that computes `SUBSTRING(sampled_at, 1, :len) AS bucket` (16 minute / 15 ten-minute / 13 hour) and groups by that COLUMN (H2 rejects `GROUP BY 1, 2`, PostgreSQL treats a re-bound parameter as a different expression), `CAST(:instance AS VARCHAR(80)) IS NULL`; new SELECT columns go at the END (`point()` reads by index: 37 `http_minutes`, 38 `delta_minutes`). The range start is aligned to a bucket boundary. Per-POD rates: counters `*_pm` = bucket sum ÷ SUM(`delta_minutes`) (never ÷ row count — the baseline row halved it and a late tick spiked it), `http_rpm` = requests ÷ SUM(`http_minutes`), rounded to THREE decimals (2 ERROR lines in an hourly bucket = 0.033/min; one decimal showed a fake 0). CLUSTER rates (client `metricsModel.podShare`, computed from the RAW totals, never the rounded server rate): Σ over pods of total × min(m, E) ÷ (m × E), E = `bucketMinutes` (a finished bucket's length, the unfinished last one's minutes passed) — summing raw per-pod rates doubled every rollout bucket, and dividing every pod by the busiest pod's minutes undercounted the others (the HTTP rotation drifts, so one pod carries 2 minutes while another carries 1); with one pod selected its own rate is shown. Shutdown-marker rows are excluded from the aggregate and from the "latest sample" read (`…AndShutdownMarkerIsNull…`). `GET /api/admin/system/metrics/overview` (`enabled`, `served_uptime_s`, pods seen in 24 h, each live pod's latest sample, last-hour totals — null, not 0, without samples) and `/series?range=1h|6h|24h|7d&instance=` (unknown range → 6h, `instance` > 80 chars → 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED`), gate `system_health.read/view`. Pod `state`: `stopped` = a shutdown marker exists (rollout / scale-down — neutral, never a warning); otherwise `live` (last sample < 3 min) or `silent` (crash, hang, cannot write — a warning however old). Two processes with the same pod name (container restart) are told apart by the id suffix, decided over the 7-day window so every screen names a pod the same way. The response carries no host / user data beyond the pod name.
- **UI:** System Health entry card → `view=metrics` (`components/admin/metrics/*`, lazy): KPI tiles from LIVE pods only (unknown = "—", neutral, never green; the queue tile takes the FULLEST pod's percentage, a sum would hide one full pod; tiles use `Tile wrapText` — label and sub-line wrap, never clip); thresholds match the main page element for element (tiles: CPU `HEALTH_THRESHOLDS.cpu`, heap `MEMORY_PCT`; charts: `HEALTH_THRESHOLDS.cpu|heap`); pod table ≥ 860 px container (headers wrap) else cards, names wrap; 15 charts per pod (safe series keys `p0…` — a pod id can't be a CSS variable name; pod colours are the non-status tokens blue / violet + mixes, never green / red / amber) or cluster totals; an empty bucket is a gap, not 0, and a value with empty neighbours is drawn as a dot (`mx-dot` — a lone point is otherwise a zero-length path); a threshold line and its legend entry show only when drawn. Only stopped pods listed (right after a rollout, before the new pod's first sample) → the pods tile is neutral; it turns red with a "samples are not being written" banner (`isNotWriting`) — never an endless "collecting" — only when the serving pod has been up longer than boot + first tick (`served_uptime_s` > `SERVING_UPTIME_GRACE_MS`, 5 min; unknown uptime → no claim) AND either no pod is listed or the newest stop is older than `FIRST_SAMPLE_GRACE_MS` (3 min). A full restart opened before the first sample therefore stays neutral. Rates (incl. the GC ms/min chart and its axis) are shown with `fmtRate` (small values keep 2–3 decimals, never rounded to 0). Sampling switched off → an "off" banner and a neutral pods tile (not "collecting", not red). A failed load says "could not be read" (pod list, charts, pods tile) — never "no pods" / "no samples"; "the last values are still shown" only when they are; a range / pod change clears the old series AND the old series failure. URL `mx_range` (default 6h not written), `mx_pod`; late responses are dropped (sequence); 60 s refresh while visible. `LogHeader`'s back button is 40 px on touch (shared with the SMTP / push log views).
- **Pod order (2026-10-17, user request: "en yeni pod en başta, canlı olan pod en önde"):** `podsSince` appends `MIN(sampled_at)` as the LAST column (index 4, marker rows counted like `MAX`) and every pod map carries `first_seen` (null on an old 4-column row). `SystemMetricsQueryService.POD_ORDER` sorts the overview pods and the `/series` pods by state (live 0 → silent 1 → stopped 2), then `first_seen` DESC (newest process first, null last), then `last_seen` DESC, then name, then id. The client mirrors it in `metricsModel.sortPods` (`PodList` + the pod picker), so an old server without `first_seen` still shows live pods first; text compares by UTF-16 code unit like Java `String.compareTo`. `seriesPods` keeps the server order (pods seen only in points go last, by name) and chart colours follow it (`p0` = the newest live pod), so colours shift when a new pod rolls out. `first_seen` is the first sample inside the 7-day name window (= retention), so two processes older than that tie and fall back to `last_seen` / name. Gates: `SystemMetricsQueryServiceTest.podOrderLiveFirstNewestFirst`, `SystemMetricSampleRepositoryTest` (first_seen), `SystemMetricSamplePostgresTest`, `metricsModel.test.js` (`sortPods`, `seriesPods`), `MetricsView.test.jsx` (list + picker order), `e2e/system-metrics.spec.js` (order).
- **Gates:** `SystemMetricSamplerTest` (HTTP span, delta minutes, shutdown marker, FunctionCounter + one turbo filter), `SystemMetricsQueryServiceTest`, `SystemMetricsControllerTest`, `SystemMetricSampleRepositoryTest` (H2), `SystemMetricSamplePostgresTest`, `HttpMetricsServiceTest.completedMinutesAfter_consumesEachMinuteOnce`, `metricsModel.test.js` (rollout bucket, rotation drift, single pod, late tick), `MetricsView.test.jsx` (incl. threshold legend), `lazyChunks.test.js`, `e2e/system-metrics.spec.js` (390/768/1280: no clipped tile text, touch targets; Turkish table headers and a lone-point dot at 1280).

### Log format, request id, tracing (2026-10-11, user decision: "prod JSON, local plain text")
- **Format:** every logback encoder is `config/logging/SwitchableLogEncoder` (`site.monitor.log.format` = `LOG_FORMAT` text|json; prod profile defaults to json, everything else text; `LOG_JSON_FORMAT` ecs|logstash|gelf → Boot `StructuredLogEncoder`). JSON without a Spring Environment or an unknown format falls back to text with a logback WARN — logging never stops. Never reintroduce a bare `PatternLayoutEncoder` (`SwitchableLogEncoderTest.shippedConfig`).
- **Request id on every line:** `CorrelationIdFilter` puts the id in MDC `requestId` for the request (restored in finally); text patterns render `${PAT_CORR}` = `rid=<id> trace=<traceId> ` (each part only when present). Pool work keeps it via `MdcTaskDecorator` (certCheckExecutor, loginIssueMailExecutor, Boot's applicationTaskExecutor through a `ThreadPoolTaskExecutorCustomizer` — deliberately NOT a global `TaskDecorator` bean, which would also hit the scheduler and pin a request id onto dynamically scheduled jobs).
- **OpenTelemetry:** `spring-boot-micrometer-tracing-opentelemetry` + OTel bridge + OTLP exporter with the JDK sender (OkHttp/Kotlin excluded; no `micrometer-registry-otlp` — metrics stay Prometheus). Spans exist always (traceId in logs, 10 % sampling), export only when `management.opentelemetry.tracing.export.otlp.endpoint` is set (Helm `config.otel.endpoint`, full `/v1/traces` URL). No Boot HTTP client builders are used, so checks to monitored sites never carry `traceparent` — keep it that way. `scripts/image-boot-check.sh` detects ERROR lines in both text and JSON. Gates: `SwitchableLogEncoderTest`, `CorrelationIdFilterTest.mdcDuringRequestOnly`, `MdcTaskDecoratorTest`, `TracingExportConfigTest`. Runbook: "Günlükler ve izleme".

### Request/response trace logging (off by default)
`RequestLoggingFilter` (`@Order(LOWEST_PRECEDENCE - 10)`) can log every HTTP request and response with method, URI, headers, body, status, and duration. It's behind `log.isTraceEnabled()` so the default `com.sitemonitor=DEBUG` level keeps it silent.
- Enable on demand: `logging.level.com.sitemonitor.config.RequestLoggingFilter=TRACE` (env or properties).
- Sensitive values are masked with `*******` via three regex patterns (JSON body / form body / URL query) covering ~60 field-name variants — EN + TR (`password|parola|sifre`, `*_password`, `token|*_token`, `secret|api_key|client_secret`, `private_key`, `session_id|jsessionid`, `pin|otp|mfa_*|verification_code`). Sensitive headers (`authorization`, `cookie`, `x-api-key`, etc.) are masked too.
- Skipped paths: `/health`, `/favicon.ico`, anything under `/assets/` or `/static/`, and common static extensions (`.js .css .map .png .svg .woff2 .ico`).
- Body log is truncated at 2000 chars.
- Bodies NEVER logged (request and response, `BODY_NEVER_LOGGED`): secret tools, login OTP, manual certs, `/api/admin/ldap`, `/api/monitoring/scripted` (env VALUES are free-named secrets), `/api/admin/user-push` (2026-10-12). JSON keys whose name ENDS with password / passwd / secret / token / api_key / apikey / private_key (e.g. `bind_password`, `webhook_secret`) are masked too (`SENSITIVE_JSON_SUFFIX_FIELDS`, bounded 64-char prefix — linear).

### Escalation & notifications
`EscalationService` selects recipients from `escalation_contacts` based on alert severity (`minAlertLevel`) and org role. `EmailNotificationService` and `WebhookService` (Teams/Slack) deliver. Every attempt logs to `notification_logs` (SENT/FAILED + error). Re-alert dedupe is daily — `EscalationServiceTest` pins this behaviour; preserve it.

**`notification_logs` columns are fitted in ONE place (2026-10-12):** `NotificationLog.fitColumns` (`@PrePersist`/`@PreUpdate`) fits every VARCHAR(255) column — recipient / CC lists keep whole addresses and end with ` … +N`, other values end with `…`. A too-long value used to fail the INSERT on PostgreSQL and the caller's try/catch dropped the trace silently. Callers need no clipping of their own; an SMTP-log resend refuses a fitted recipient list (`RECIPIENTS_TRUNCATED`). Storm RESOLVE mails also reach the members' stamped on-call addresses (`alert_events.oncall_emails`, `StormService.planOncall`, review round 4): an on-call address joins a team's storm mail only if it received the opening of EVERY member of that dispatch; otherwise it gets a separate mail listing only the members it received (team isolation — another team's target names never leak), and each member's resolution reaches an address once across dispatches (co-owned SY + UG members). Round 5: an address that is already a recipient of the team mail of the dispatch that reports a member is seeded as "reported" (`seedTeamMail`), so it never gets a second on-call mail; an inventory row whose UG team equals its SY team is ONE dispatch (`resolveDispatches`), not two. Gates: `NotificationLogFitColumnsTest`, `SmtpLogNocRedactionTest.truncatedRecipientsAreNotResent`, `StormSilentMemberRecoveryTest.recoveryMailReachesOncallWhoGotTheAlarm|oncallSeesOnlyTheMembersItGot|partialOncallGetsSeparateMail`.

**Recipient routing (team vs escalation)** — two helpers, `isStandaloneMon(alertType)` + `includeManagerContacts(alertType, level)`, govern three paths (initial alert `processConfirmedOutage`, resolution `sendResolutionNotification`, manual resend `reNotify`) and MUST stay consistent across them:
- Standalone monitoring alerts (keyword/ping/http/`TYPE_DOMAIN_EXPIRY`/all `DOMAINMON_*`) resolve the team from `AlertEvent.teamId` (stamped at alarm creation) — **not** the certificate inventory. Standalone domain monitors aren't in `certificate_inventory`, so resolving their team from inventory lost the owning team (the "resend went to the müdür, not the team" bug — fixed by routing all three paths through `event.getTeamId()`).
- **Escalation contacts come ONLY from the alarm's owning team(s)** (product decision 2026-09-28) — single source `EscalationContactScope.forOwners(repo, level, syTeamId, ugTeamId)`: SY and UG each contribute only THEIR OWN contacts (union; one mail per address, webhooks deduped by URL); `teamId == null` → empty, repository never queried. There is **no global fallback**: if a team has no contact at that level the alarm goes to the team's own recipients only (team address / notification group / push) plus a WARN log. Another team's manager or contact must never be added, and teamless contacts are never recipients (cannot be created — 400). The old "fall back to global contacts" path used unscoped `findByActiveTrue…` / `findByMinAlertLevel…` queries that returned EVERY team's contacts (cross-team leak, v3.0.0 → v20.89.0); `EscalationContactScopeTest` is the source gate, `EscalationContactLeakTest` the behaviour gate. The team manager (`teams.manager_id` / LDAP) is NOT an automatic alarm recipient.
- **Acting on an alarm follows ownership (2026-10-09, user decision).** `AlertOwnership` (predicate `EscalationService.isStandaloneEvent`): a standalone alarm is owned ONLY by `AlertEvent.teamId`; inventory-routed alarms (cert, ACCESSIBILITY, unstamped derived Port/DNS) by the stamp + inventory SY/UG. Ack, resolve, re-notify, recipient preview, bulk and incident comments use it; rows carry `act_scope` so the UI hides actions. READ scope is unchanged (JPQL lists). Gate: `AlertStandaloneOwnerScopeTest`.
- **Level gate (2026-09-19, all monitor types):** `includeManagerContacts` = `!WARNING` — WARNING alarms of standalone monitors are team-only; HIGH/CRITICAL add the owning teams' escalation contacts by their `minAlertLevel`.
- **Standalone vs inventory routing:** standalone monitors (HTTP/Keyword/Ping/Page/Scripted/PageSpeed/Domain and user-added Port/DNS — `standalone` flag / team stamp in context) take their team ONLY from the event/monitor, never from the inventory. Cert alerts, ACCESSIBILITY and inventory-DERIVED Port/DNS (context deliberately unstamped — `SchedulerService.alarmTeamOf`) route like the inventory: SY + UG, each team with its own contacts. Ownerless alarms (no team after full resolution) send nothing on any channel. `TeamAdminService.moveAll` re-stamps OPEN alarm events; a team with open alarms or SY/UG-bound records cannot be deleted (409).
- **Manual "Check now" never raises an alarm** (product decision 2026-09-29, prod incident: a scoped manager's bulk "Check Now (39)" on Synthetic opened alarms and an org-wide storm push). Every trigger endpoint records the result, then evaluates with `handleSweepResults(type, items, manual=true)` — which ONLY runs `reconcileRecoveries`: no new alarm, no confirmation chain, no escalation / re-alert / storm, no suppression bookkeeping; a healthy result may close the open alarm. The next scheduled sweep opens alarms under the normal rules. `evaluateDomainAlarmsNow` is the MANUAL entry; the scheduled critical-domain check uses `evaluateDomainAlarms(m, r, false)`. A manual check also never sends a domain threshold reminder (`DomainExpiryReminderService` runs only from the daily/critical sweeps), never trips `ScriptedAnomalyGuard` (`evaluate(m, res, manual)`; the timeout streak counts scheduled runs only — `scripted_checks.manual`), and never consumes DNS change detection (manual rows are stored with `dns_records.manual = true`; the sweep baseline is `findLatestScheduledSuccessful`; domain registration likewise: manual/live rows and confirmation/recovery rechecks (`checkRecheck`) are stored with `domain_checks.manual = true` (out of band), `DomainCheckerService.checkManual`, scheduled baseline `findLatestScheduledWithData`; the Registration tab reads stored data on open and only runs a live lookup on Refresh; manual DNS rows are never change events). Gates: `ManualCheckNoAlarmTest`, `ManualCheckWiringTest`, `ManualCheckMarkerQueriesTest`. List rows carry `can_check` (= `SessionScope.canOperateTeam` on the effective team) and the UI counts/queues only those.
- **Alert storms are TEAM-scoped** (same decision): scope key `TEAM:<id>` (per-group: `TEAM:<id>|GROUP:<g>`); the window count, threshold (COUNT, or PERCENT of the team's own active monitors — `totalActiveMonitorsForTeam`), root cause and every notification come from the owning team's events only; ownerless events never storm. Each dispatch (SY, and UG for co-owned inventory rows) shows ONLY its own count/name/root cause — there is no "Tüm monitörler" / org-wide storm; legacy `ACCOUNT`/group storms are disbanded by `lifecycleSweep`. Storm push level = the highest member level (`stormPushLevel`), never wider than the individual alarms' audience. The threshold counts DISTINCT targets (`distinctTargets` — one host's ACCESSIBILITY/PORT/DNS/HTTP alerts are one target); PERCENT has an absolute floor of 3 (`PERCENT_MIN_TARGETS`). Legacy storms stay inert for `site.monitor.storm.legacy-retire-grace-minutes` (15) after start-up, then `retireLegacy` moves still-down members into their team storm silently (above threshold) or releases them as already notified (`releaseFromStormAsNotified`) — never individual INITIALs; the recovery of recovered members goes only to their own team. Gate: `StormTeamIsolationTest`.
- **Storms have a bounded lifetime and every silent decision leaves a trace (2026-09-30, prod incident: the SY team's 3 permanently failing synthetic tests kept a team storm above the hysteresis floor forever, so `StormService.evaluate` attached EVERY new DOWN alarm of that team silently — no email, push, webhook, NOC, `notification_logs` row or push-decision row; the UI showed "0 notifications").** `alert_storms.last_member_at` is stamped at creation and on every attach; once `site.monitor.storm.quiet-minutes` (default 5, 5–1440, Settings → Alert Storm → Quiet Window) pass without a new member the storm is SEALED: `evaluate` sends individually instead of attaching and `lifecycleOne` resolves it regardless of still-down count. On resolve, still-down members announced in the storm's last mail (`createdAt ≤ storm.lastReAlertAt`) are released as notified (`releaseFromStormAsNotified`), unannounced ones are unlinked (`lastReAlertAt=null` → individual INITIAL on the next sweep). The SUPPRESSED branch of `processConfirmedOutage` calls `recordStormSuppression` (log row trigger `STORM`, status `SKIPPED: fırtına #N …`, push row `SKIPPED_STORM`); the ownerless gate writes `SKIPPED: takım yok` + `SKIPPED_NO_TEAM`; `sendCombinedAlert` no longer returns early when mail is skipped and logs `SKIPPED: alıcı yok` / `SKIPPED: e-posta kanalı kapalı`; storm mails are logged per member alarm (`STORM_INITIAL` / `STORM_REALERT` / `STORM_RESOLVE`). The alert list/detail show a `StormBadge` (`storm_id`) and the timeline renders the `STORM` row as "notification handed over to the storm". `StormSuppressionBackfill` writes the trace once for historical storm members with no log rows. Never reintroduce a silent, unlogged suppression path — a notification that is not sent must say why in `notification_logs`. Gates: `StormSealingTest`, `EscalationServiceTest.processConfirmedOutage_stormSuppressed_leavesTrace`, `StormSuppressionBackfillTest`, `AlertHistoryStorm.test.jsx`.
- **Storm observability (2026-09-30).** `alert_storms` freezes an opening snapshot (`team_id`, `group_name`, `threshold_unit/value/effective`, `window_minutes`, `quiet_minutes`, `targets_at_open`, `peak_targets`, `trigger_event_id`) IN the atomic promotion INSERT (`insertStormIfAbsent(…, OpenSnapshot)`, 17 bind args, the first 7 keep their order — a separate UPDATE let a concurrent lifecycle `save` null them) and a `resolve_reason` (`FLOOR`/`SEALED`/`DISABLED`/`LEGACY_RETIRE`). Membership is durable in `alert_storm_members` (`AlertStormMember`: join kind TRIGGER/PEER/ATTACH/LEGACY, `announced_at`, leave kind RECOVERED/NOTIFIED/UNLINKED/MOVED/RELEASED) because `alert_events.storm_id` is cleared on close. Writes are JDBC on the alarm path and must stay O(1) round trips per event: opening = one `batchUpdate(SQL_MEMBER_INSERT)` (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, no exists-check), attach = one INSERT, announce = one UPDATE (IN chunks of 500), close = one `batchUpdate(SQL_MEMBER_LEFT)`; every recording call is try/catch — observability must never change or delay a notification decision. The in-flight lifecycle refresh uses a targeted `UPDATE … member_count, peak_targets = GREATEST(…)` instead of `stormRepo.save` (a stale entity save rewound `last_member_at`). Read side: `StormStatusService` + `StormStatusController` (`GET /api/monitoring/storm/status|history|analytics|{id}`, gate `alerts.read/view`, scope like `TodayPanelController.openAlerts`; `status` is memoized 10 s per scope key, `?fresh=1` bypasses; per-team active monitor counts come from ONE grouped query `StormService.activeMonitorsByTeam()` (60 s), triggers/live members are batch-loaded — never add a per-team or per-storm query to these polled paths) → `?tab=storms` (`StormStatusPage`, URL prefix `sf_`) and the Settings → Alert Storm live panel (`StormLivePanel`). Adding a storm column/table: patch it in `applySchemaPatches`, append new INSERT columns at the END (`StormTeamIsolationTest` reads the bind args positionally), register retention (`alert-storm-members-orphan`). Gates: `StormObservabilityRecordTest`, `StormStatusServiceTest`, `StormStatusControllerTest`, `StormObservabilityQueriesTest`, `StormStatusPage.test.jsx`, `StormLivePanel.test.jsx`.
- **Optional: push not handed over to storms (2026-10-03; `site.monitor.storm.push-individual`, group `storm`, DEFAULT FALSE since 2026-10-04 by user decision: push is handed over to the storm as before).** When TRUE, a storm groups e-mail (plus webhook / NOC) only; each member alarm's push goes out individually, in alarm order.
  - **Joining a storm:** the `EscalationService` SUPPRESSED branch sends the OPEN push via `pushStormMember`. The STORM row says `… (push tek tek)` (`STATUS_STORM_MAIL_ONLY_SUFFIX`; the prefix stays), and no `SKIPPED_STORM` row is written.
  - **Storm-member level raise:** sends an ESCALATION push.
  - **Daily re-alert:** `StormService.enqueueStormPush` DAILY_REALERT queues one `enqueueAlert("DAILY_REALERT")` per open, unacknowledged member, ordered by createdAt then id.
  - **Storm INITIAL / RESOLVE:** the aggregated push is not sent, unless a storm notice was already SENT (`UserPushService.stormNoticeSent`, covers a mid-storm switch). Member resolves go through `enqueueResolve`, whose symmetry now finds the SENT OPEN row.
  - **Quiet hours:** team quiet hours apply via `TeamQuietHoursService.defersPush` → `SKIPPED_TEAM_QUIET`, with no digest item.
  - **Other rules:** cap / maintenance / realert-off / dedupe come from `enqueueAlert`.
  - **Flag false (default):** byte-identical to the pre-2026-10-03 behaviour (`SKIPPED_STORM` + aggregated `storm:*` push).
  - **Gates:** `StormPushIndividualTest`, `EscalationServiceTest.processConfirmedOutage_stormSuppressed_leavesTrace` (parametrised), `StormTeamIsolationTest` (fixture pins OFF), `StormSettings.test.jsx`.
- **Storm push ↔ alarm link (2026-10-04, user request).** In the default mode an alarm's push is handed over to the storm (`SKIPPED_STORM`), but the alarm history still shows which aggregated storm push covered it, when, and to whom.
  - **Recording:** `UserPushService.enqueueStormNoticeLocalized` writes the member alarms each storm push covers into `storm_push_coverage`:
    - columns: `push_key` = delivery `dedupe_key`, `team_id`; UNIQUE(push_key, alert_event_id);
    - opening / daily push: the members at that moment; resolve push: only the recovered members, the same set as the `STORM_RESOLVE` mail log.
  - **Write rules:** one `batchUpdate(StormPushCoverageService.SQL_INSERT)` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, try/catch + WARN, after the decision (sent rows or skip row), so it never changes the push.
  - **Isolation:** a TEAM:A push never covers a TEAM:B alarm. Individual push mode records nothing, except the resolve push after a mid-storm switch.
  - **Reads:** `StormPushCoverageService`, a fixed number of queries per page.
    - `GET /api/admin/alerts/{id}/storm-push` (same gate as `/push-deliveries`);
    - Push bildirimlerim (`storm_push` / `storm_alarms`);
    - push log detail (`storm_coverage` / `storm_pushes` / `storm_awaiting`);
    - storm detail `notifications.storm_pushes`.
  - **Legacy:** pushes with no recorded row are estimated from the `alert_storm_members` window (`inferred: true`); a push with any recorded row is never estimated.
  - **Pending:** `pending` means the push was handed over, the storm is active, the alarm hasn't left, and no opening/daily push covers it yet.
  - **Retention:** `storm-push-coverage-orphan`.
  - **Gates:** `StormPushCoverageRecordTest`, `StormPushCoverageQueriesTest`, `AlertStormPushEndpointTest`, `PushLogStormLinkTest`, `AlertStormPush.test.jsx`, `PushHistoryStormLinks.test.jsx`.
- **Auto-close is reconciliation, not a transition** (2026-09-29, "ghost alarm" fix): `MonitoringOutageService.handleSweepDomain` decides per sweep from "open alarm in DB + current result healthy"; every healthy result increments a consecutive counter and N consecutive healthy sweeps close the alarm (atomic, idempotent; the active recovery chain is only a fast path, stuck chains are replaced). Fleet-wide outage suppression (`noteSuppression`) and `site.monitor.<type>.alert-enabled=false` may ONLY stop new alarms / confirmation / re-alerts — never recovery (`reconcileRecoveries`). A sibling monitor sharing the event key (`domain|alertType`) that is still DOWN keeps the alarm open. `DNS_CHANGED` / `DOMAINMON_CHANGED` close manually by design. Cert alarms reconcile even under outage suspicion / disabled expiry alerts (`resolveVerifiedStaleCertAlerts`).
- Email **content** (detail table) for `DOMAINMON_*` is rebuilt fresh from the latest `DomainCheck` via `reconstructDomainContext(domain)` — `latestCheckRepo` is cert-only and returns nothing for a domain monitor, which is why resend/resolution mails looked empty before.
- Domain expiry alarm **severity** must mirror the card status tiers (`DomainCheckerService`: `days≤crit→CRITICAL`, `crit<days≤warn→WARNING`) — set in `SchedulerService.addDomainSweepItems`. Don't reintroduce a flat `HIGH`.
- **Noise analysis suggestions are a code catalogue (2026-10-01).** `AlertNoiseService` classifies noisy targets/teams and emits `suggestions[]` with codes from `AlertNoiseSuggestion` (thresholds are the service's `static final` constants — one place); the UI renders `t('noise.sug.<CODE>.title|body', ...params)` and turns `action.kind` (`open_monitor|open_alerts|open_settings|open_maintenance`) into a button (`alerts/noiseModel.js#runSuggestionAction`). Adding a code means adding TR + EN `noise.sug.<CODE>.title` / `.body` in the same change — `AlertNoiseSuggestionI18nGateTest` reads `frontend/src/i18n/index.jsx` and fails the backend build otherwise (the frontend used-keys gate cannot see the dynamic key). The response is additive: never rename the legacy fields (`top`, `flapping`, `heat`, `series`, `by_type`, KPI totals) — the panel and its old test depend on them.

- **Quiet hours are opt-in and never silent (2026-10-01, approved proposal 15).** Team window = `teams.quiet_start/quiet_end/quiet_days/quiet_min_level` (HH:mm Europe/Istanbul, may cross midnight, days = the day the window STARTS, null = no window). Rule lives in `QuietHours` (also used for personal push quiet hours on `app_users.push_quiet_*`). `EscalationService.sendCombinedAlert` defers only INITIAL/ESCALATION/DAILY_REALERT (`TeamQuietHoursService.DEFERRABLE_TRIGGERS`) for the owning team(s) in their window: default only WARNING, `quiet_min_level=CRITICAL` also HIGH, CRITICAL never; MANUAL resend, NOC and storm mails are not deferred. Every deferral writes `notification_logs` trigger `QUIET_HOURS` + `SKIPPED: sessiz saat …` and a `quiet_digest_items` row (UNIQUE alarm×team×window); push gets `SKIPPED_TEAM_QUIET` via the `quiet_deferred` ctx flag.
