A resolution whose OPENING was deferred and whose digest hasn't gone is folded into the digest. `QuietDigestService` (minute job, `scheduler_lock` `quiet-digest`, claim-token = at-most-once) mails one digest per team per recipient set, logs `QUIET_DIGEST` per alarm and restamps `last_re_alert_at` of still-open alarms; timed escalation steps are held while quiet/pending and `QUIET_DIGEST` is a step anchor. Settings come from a 60 s in-memory cache (no per-alarm query; other pods see a change within 60 s). Gates: `QuietHoursEscalationTest` (no-config ≡ today on every path), `QuietDigestServiceTest`, `EscalationStepQuietHoldTest`, `UserPushQuietHoursTest`.
- **Timed escalation steps + per-family re-alert (2026-10-02, approved proposal 10) are opt-in.** `escalation_contacts.delay_minutes` (null/0 = today exactly): a delayed contact is left out of every immediate send and is notified ONCE (email + own webhook, subject `[ESKALASYON · N dk onaysız]`) by `EscalationStepService` (fixedDelay `site.monitor.escalation.step-interval-ms`, `scheduler_lock` key `escalation-steps`) when the alarm is still open AND unacknowledged after N minutes from its latest INITIAL/ESCALATION/QUIET_DIGEST anchor; dedupe = `alert_escalation_steps` UNIQUE (alarm, contact, level), claimed before sending. All routing rules still apply (owning teams only, level gate, WARNING-standalone → no contacts, ownerless → nothing); ack / storm / maintenance / fleet-outage skips write `ESCALATION_STEP` + `SKIPPED: …`. Re-alert override keys `site.monitor.realert.<family>-minutes` (0 = global interval; 15–10080). Gates: `EscalationStepRegressionTest` (no-config ≡ today incl. notification_logs), `EscalationStepServiceTest`, `ReAlertIntervalOverrideTest`.
- **Runbook note + incident link (2026-10-02, proposal 14).** If a non-blank `MonitorGuide` exists for the alarm's type+target, `sendCombinedAlert` appends a "Ne yapılmalı" block (markdown → escaped plain text, ≤ 1000 chars email / ≤ 300 webhook) — INITIAL/ESCALATION/re-alert/manual resend and the timed step only; resolution, storm, NOC and personal push are unchanged. Without a guide the output is byte-identical (`RunbookNoteNotificationTest`). Incidents carry `incident_records.alert_event_id`; the alarm detail's "Olay kaydı aç" opens the existing `IncidentFormModal` prefilled (`incidents.manage/edit`), `GET /api/incidents/by-alert/{id}` shows the link afterwards.
- **HTTP advanced request + `HTTP_SLOW` (2026-10-02, proposal 9).** `http_monitors` gained encrypted `custom_headers_enc` (global admin only, like Keyword/PageSpeed), `basic_auth_user/pass_enc`, `request_body`/`request_content_type` (POST only, ≤ 64 KB), `json_path`/`json_expected` (small resolver `service/http/JsonAssertion`, failure = HTTP_DOWN with "JSON doğrulaması başarısız: …") and `slow_response_enabled`/`slow_threshold_ms` → new alert type `HTTP_SLOW` (wired like `KEYWORD_SLOW`; never a storm member). With no advanced field set the old 7-argument `HttpCheckerService.check` runs and the request is byte-identical (`HttpRequestBuildTest`); headers/auth/body are dropped on cross-host redirects. A new monitor alert type must be added everywhere `KEYWORD_SLOW`/`HTTP_SLOW` appear (grep both).
- **Push outbox is claimed, not just read (2026-10-02, proposal 3).** `UserPushService.drainOutbox` leases due PENDING rows with `claimDue` (writes a per-run unique lease stamp into `next_attempt_at`, ≥ 2 min) and sends only `findByIdInAndNextAttemptAtOrderByIdAsc(ids, lease)` — two pods never send the same push; a dead pod's lease expires and the row is retried. If the claim query fails the old read-and-send path runs.
- **Personal push: overflow summary, preferences, language, history, escalation-step push (2026-10-04, user-approved proposals 2–6).** Links in pushes are unreachable (the push app runs outside the company network), so never add links or one-tap actions to push texts.
  - **Overflow summary:** rows over the cap stay `RATE_LIMITED`. They are linked through `overflow_summary_id` to ONE `OVERFLOW_SUMMARY` row by `UserPushOverflowService` (lock `push-overflow`, conditional-UPDATE claim).
    - The summary is exempt from the cap.
    - Personal quiet hours or snooze defer it, unless its level gets through.
    - Opt-out, inactive account or maintenance writes a trace row instead.
  - **Critical bypass:** `critical-bypass-cap` (default off) exempts CRITICAL alarm, storm and escalation-step pushes from the cap.
  - **Preferences:** `app_users.push_min_level`, `push_families`, `push_snooze_until`, `push_snooze_critical`, `push_lang`.
    - Skips are always written as rows: `SKIPPED_USER_TYPE` / `SKIPPED_USER_LEVEL` / `SKIPPED_USER_SNOOZE`.
    - RESOLVE and `sendDirect` (login codes) are exempt.
    - With no preferences set, behaviour is identical to before.
    - There is no favourites-only filter: cert and inventory-derived alarms cannot be mapped to favourites reliably.
  - **Language:** messages are built per recipient language (`PushI18n`); Turkish output is byte-identical, and batch ids are split per language.
    - Team, storm and direct notices take `LocalizedText` through the `*Localized` methods.
    - Add English text whenever you add a push text.
    - The alarm's own reason text (`{neden}`) stays Turkish.
  - **History:** `/api/me/push-history` returns only the user's own rows plus decision rows for teams the user belongs to (never other teams).
  - **Self-service:** `/api/me/push-preferences|push-snooze|push-test`, audited. `PUSH_*` events are in the INTEGRATION category.
  - **Escalation-step push:** the contact resolves to a single active user (`user_id`, otherwise a unique e-mail match); dedupe `ESC_STEP:<contact>:<level>`; `site.monitor.escalation.step-push-enabled` (true).
  - **Schema:** `push_trigger` is VARCHAR(40); "DOMAIN_EXPIRY_REMINDER" did not fit 20 and those rows were lost silently on PostgreSQL.

**On-call rotation (2026-10-11, user decision: "takım seçerse nöbetçiye de").** `oncall_schedules` (one per team: enabled, DAILY|WEEKLY, `anchor` = first handover as Istanbul local `yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm`, ordered `member_ids` ≤ 50, `notify_oncall` default OFF) + `oncall_overrides` (range → person; newest wins; pruned 180 days after end). `service/oncall/OnCallRotation` is the pure calculator (fixed UTC+3, bounded loops); `OnCallService.notifyTargets(teams)` returns the CURRENT on-call of teams with `notify_oncall` on (active accounts only, 30 s memo per team set, failure → empty). `EscalationService.oncallFor(sy, ug, level)` adds them ONLY for HIGH/CRITICAL: `sendCombinedAlert` (after contacts, same `seen`/`excludeEmails` dedupe; quiet-deferred teams contribute none) + `UserPushService.enqueueOnCall` (same channel/personal-pref/cap rules as the escalation-step push, skipped when the person already got the team push for that trigger — for a manual resend: a team `RESEND:` row for that person in the last 2 min — dedupe key `ONCALL:…`), `previewReNotify` (kind `ONCALL`) + the resend push preview (`UserPushService.preview(id, team, oncall)`) and the manual-resend count. The RESOLUTION mail goes to the on-call addresses that actually received the opening/escalation/re-alert (`alert_events.oncall_emails`, read-only in the entity, written only by `AlertEventRepository.setOncallEmails`) — never to whoever is on call at resolution time (2026-10-12). Overrides need a saved schedule and apply only while it is enabled (same as notifications and the Teams row). The alarm detail asks the on-call of the alarm's OWNER teams (`owner_team_ids` on enriched rows = `AlertOwnership.ownerTeamIds`; a standalone alarm never shows the inventory SY/UG on-call). Option off / no schedule / service missing → byte-identical (`EscalationOnCallTest`). Endpoints `/api/oncall/teams/{id}` (read: `oncall.read` + `canViewMonitoring`; write: `oncall.manage` + `canManage`), `/overrides`, `/api/oncall/current?teamIds=`; audit `ONCALL_*`. Shown on the Teams row ("Nöbetçi"), the team menu → `admin/oncall/OnCallScheduleModal`, the alarm detail (`alerts/AlertOnCall`, open alarms) and the 7/24 call sheet (`oncall` with phone). Team delete removes the schedule. Gates: `OnCallRotationTest`, `OnCallServiceTest`, `OnCallControllerTest`, `EscalationOnCallTest`, `oncallModel.test.js`, `OnCallScheduleModal.test.jsx`, `e2e/oncall.spec.js`.

**Critical-domain second daily check** — `SchedulerService.runCriticalDomainChecks()` (`@Scheduled` cron `DOMAIN_CRITICAL_CHECK_CRON`, default `0 0 16 * * *` Europe/Istanbul; separate `scheduler_lock` key `domain-critical-sweep`) re-checks ONLY active domains whose last check `days_remaining ≤ DOMAIN_CRITICAL_CHECK_THRESHOLD_DAYS` (default 7). It calls the same `evaluateDomainAlarmsNow`, so a same-day renewal auto-closes the open `DOMAINMON_EXPIRY` alarm without waiting for the next morning sweep, while the daily re-alert dedupe still suppresses a second notification for a still-critical domain. Toggle with `DOMAIN_CRITICAL_CHECK_ENABLED`. Two same-day checks intentionally write two `domain_checks` rows.

### Frontend shape
- Single-page app in `App.jsx` — `tab` state (synced to `?tab=<key>` and validated against a whitelist) switches between Dashboard / All Certs / Stats / Warnings / Renewal (+ Renewal Guide) / Forecast / Inventory (`domains`) / Activity / MyActivity / AlertHistory / WeakAlgo / Uptime / Port / DNS / Weekly Reports / Incident History / Admin / Permissions / Settings (`AdminSettings`) / SystemHealth / AuditLog (`system`) / SQL Playground / Help. No React Router. Several tabs are visibility-gated: Permissions & SQL Playground (global admin), Settings (bootstrap `admin` user), AuditLog (global admin or AUDIT role).
- API client is a flat `api.*` object in `api/client.js` — `fetch` with `credentials: 'include'`. A 401 after a successful login (flagged via `sessionStorage.cm.session.active`) forces `window.location.assign('/?session=expired')`. The initial bootstrap 401 returns `null` and lets `App.jsx` render `<Login>`.
- i18n: `useT(key)` hook + provider in `i18n/index.jsx`; dictionaries live in `i18n/tr.js` (static, in the entry chunk) and `i18n/en.js` (lazy chunk, 2026-10-02 bundle split). `index.jsx` re-exports `TR` but NOT `EN` (importing EN statically would pull it back into the entry — tests import `../i18n/en.js` directly). EN at startup: `main.jsx` preloads it and `LangProvider` shows `LanguageBootSplash` until it arrives; runtime TR→EN switches only after `loadLanguage('en')` resolves (busy state on the switch); a failed load keeps TR, toasts `lang.loadFailed` and forces `X-Lang: tr` for the session. Add new keys to BOTH files. The parity test (`i18n-parity.test.jsx`) enforces both languages have every key, no empty values, matching placeholder counts.
- **Themes (2026-10-05, user decision).** Eight themes: Light and Dark (base) plus Blueprint, Parchment, Alloy, Obsidian, Slag and Crucible.
  - **Single source:** `theme/themes.js` and `ThemeCatalog.java`, kept in the same order by `ThemeCatalogSyncTest` and `theme-catalog-sync.test.js`.
  - **DOM:** `<html data-theme="<id>" data-scheme="light|dark">` + `color-scheme`, switched via `useTheme()` in `i18n/theme.jsx`.
  - **CSS keys on the SCHEME:** the Tailwind `dark:` variant and every dark rule in `App.css` / `globals.css` use `[data-scheme="dark"]`. Light tokens are `:root, [data-scheme="light"]`. Extra themes ONLY override tokens in `styles/themes.css`, imported after `App.css`.
  - **Rules:** never write a theme-specific component rule; never use `dark:` inside a nested `data-theme` preview; in JS ask `useTheme().isDark` / `scheme`, never `theme === 'dark'`. Hardcoded Tailwind palette colours (`bg-blue-50` …) only follow light/dark.
  - **Contrast gate:** `themeContrast.test.js` requires text ≥ 4.5:1, ring ≥ 3:1 and distinct status hues for every theme.
  - **Adding a theme:** update `themes.js`, `ThemeCatalog`, a `themes.css` block, `themeLabels.js`, and add `theme.name.*` / `theme.desc.*` in TR and EN.
  - **Admin:** Settings → Görünüm → Temalar (`?sec=themes`, `ThemeSettingsController`, `/api/admin/themes`, audit `THEME_SETTINGS_SAVE`).
    - Keys `site.monitor.theme.enabled` (CSV) and `site.monitor.theme.default` (`system` or an enabled id) are GLOBAL_ONLY; a scoped admin sees the page read-only.
    - Validation is also enforced in `AppSettingsService`: known ids, at least one enabled, default must be enabled, `system` needs light + dark.
    - `/api/branding` returns `themes: {enabled, default}`.
  - **Per-user choice:** localStorage `site-monitor-theme`, synced through user preferences (`LOCAL_PREF_KEYS` / `UserPreferencesService.THEME_LOCAL_KEY`; unknown values are ignored).
    - At login the server wins and `useThemePrefSync` applies it without a reload.
    - A disabled or unknown choice falls back to the admin default, but the stored choice is kept.
  - **Preview:** tab-only via `startPreview`, with `ThemePreviewBar`.
- **Scoped admin ("müdür", AD ADMIN with `viewTeamIds` set) and Settings (2026-09-10 product decision).** The Settings tab is gated by `systemRole === 'ADMIN'` (global *or* scoped); `AdminSettings` takes `globalAdmin` and renders a "global administrators only" notice instead of the four secret-bearing sections (SMTP, LDAP, Secret Decryptor, Database) when it is false. Backend mirrors this exactly: those four controllers keep `SessionScope.requireNotScopedAdmin`; General/Branding/LoginAnomaly/Storm/UserPush accept the scoped admin, and `AppSettingsService.save` rejects `AppSettingsCatalog.GLOBAL_ONLY` keys (SSRF allow-*, DNS resolvers, CA bundle/TOFU, k6 binary/API, push URL/headers, base-url, CORS, log levels) with 403 for a scoped admin no matter which controller called it; `getCatalogForClient` marks them `global_only`/`read_only` so the UI disables them. `SettingsScopedAdminGateTest` pins the controller split and the key list — add a new secret-bearing setting to `GLOBAL_ONLY`, never rely on the UI lock alone.
- **Server messages follow the UI language (2026-09-10).** `api/client.js` sends `X-Lang: tr|en` (read from `LANG_STORAGE_KEY`) on every request; backend `util/Msg.t(tr, en)` resolves it (X-Lang → Accept-Language → `tr`; no request context → `tr`). Any `"message"`/`IllegalArgumentException` text a settings page shows as a toast must go through `Msg.t` — never a bare Turkish literal (that was QA ISSUE-001: English UI, Turkish toast). Audit details and log lines stay Turkish on purpose; `MsgTest` pins the resolution order.
- **UI library: shadcn/ui is the project default (product decision, 2026-09-25).** Every new or changed UI element is built from the components at https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components, which live in `frontend/src/components/shadcn/` (added with `npx shadcn add`, styled with Tailwind v4). Do not write new elements with the legacy App.css classes (`.btn`, `.input`, `.modal-box`, `.badge-*`), and don't restyle a legacy element to *look like* shadcn. Convert the legacy elements you touch. If shadcn has no direct match, compose shadcn components; only as a last resort write Tailwind in the shadcn style. Read `frontend/docs/SHADCN.md` before any UI work. It covers the component catalogue, which component to use for which need, the project's adaptations and the testing pitfalls. The adaptations are: React 18 needs `forwardRef` on components used as an `asChild` child; the shadcn CLI emits a broken `cn` import; App.css is unlayered, so it beats Tailwind. Tests should query by role/name or `data-slot`, not by class. The project uses the *Radix* variants (read `ui.shadcn.com/docs/components/radix/<name>`; the docs site now defaults to Base UI pages, and `shadcn init` must never be re-run). The `shadcnOnly.test.js` gate counts raw HTML controls and hand-rolled widgets per file against a baseline that may only shrink (`npm run shadcn:baseline`).
- **Mobile web is a requirement (product decision, 2026-09-26).** Every screen must be fully usable on phones (360–414 px) and tablets (768 px). Build mobile-first with Tailwind breakpoints (`sm`/`md`/`lg`), with no fixed pixel widths on content, wrapping toolbars, tables that scroll or turn into cards, modals that fit the screen, and touch targets of at least 40 px. Verify at 390×844 and 768×1024 with `e2e/responsive.spec.js`; jsdom proves nothing about layout. Full rules: `frontend/docs/RESPONSIVE.md`.
- **Cards and detail windows never get a coloured left stripe (product decision, 2026-09-26).** No `before:` bar, no `border-l-*` status colour, no inset left shadow. Status is shown by the status `Badge` and `data-status`.
- Icons: `lucide-react` only — never emoji.
- **404, page meta, favicon (2026-10-08, user request).**
  - **Routing:** `config/SpaNotFoundAdvice` (ordered before `GlobalExceptionHandler`) answers `NoResourceFoundException`: an unknown page (GET/HEAD, accepts HTML) gets `index.html` with HTTP 404, an unknown `/api/**` a JSON 404 (`code:"NOT_FOUND"`), a missing asset (`/assets/`, `/brand/`, `/fonts/`, known extensions) a plain-text 404 — never HTML, always `no-store` (a missing asset used to inherit the 1-year immutable cache header).
  - **Frontend:** `utils/appRoutes.js` is the single source of `VALID_TABS` (`isKnownAppPath`, `tabFromSearch`, `NOT_FOUND_TAB`). `main.jsx` lazy-loads `pages/NotFoundPage` for any path other than `/` / `index.html` BEFORE the auth bootstrap (public, branded, all themes). An unknown `?tab=` shows `notfound/NotFoundPanel` in-app; a tab the role cannot open shows "Erişim yok" (used to be blank); a stale saved landing tab still falls back to the dashboard.
  - **Meta:** `hooks/usePageMeta` + `utils/pageMeta.js` are the ONLY writers of `document.title` ("<Sayfa> · <brand>", white-label `tab_title`/`app_name` as suffix) and the description meta, for every tab and the login / session / passive / maintenance / password-change / 404 / no-access states. A new tab needs `meta.tab.<id>.title|description` in TR + EN (`pageMeta-gate.test.js`: title ≤ 60, description 50–160, unique, translated; also compares `responsive.spec` tabs with `VALID_TABS`).
  - **Favicon:** `branding/assets/favicon.svg` is a small-size rendering of the SAME turnip mark (body `#813387`, leaves `#64A64F`); `node branding/tools/make_favicon.mjs` (Playwright Chromium rasteriser) regenerates `favicon.ico` (16/32/48), `favicon-16/32.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `icon-192/512.png`, maskable 512 and `site.webmanifest` (served as `application/manifest+json`); `make_variants.py` no longer writes the favicon. `useStatusFavicon('ok')` leaves the favicon alone. `brand-default.test.jsx` pins sizes, palette, ICO frames and public/ ↔ branding/assets parity.
  - **Live tour:** `e2e/live/site-tour.live.spec.js` visits every tab at 1280 + 390 against the real backend (no 5xx, no raw keys, localized title/description, no overflow, heading visible) + the 404 contract. Run it after cross-cutting UI changes.
- **Error contract (2026-10-08, user request: "hata mesajları çok açıklayıcı olsun").**
  - **Backend:** every error body is `{success:false, code, error (Msg.t, what · why · next step), request_id}`; `CorrelationIdFilter` honours a safe incoming `X-Request-Id` (≤ 40 chars) or generates one, returns it in the header and logs `[istek=<id>]` on 500s. `GlobalExceptionHandler` never leaks exception text (unexpected 500 → generic friendly text + log); stable codes for validation (`VALIDATION_FAILED` + `fields`), DB constraint classes (`DUPLICATE_RECORD`, `REFERENCED_RECORD`, `MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD`, `VALUE_TOO_LONG`), `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (+ `max_mb`), `CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION`, `DB_UNAVAILABLE`, etc. Existing domain codes (DOMAIN_EXISTS, MAINTENANCE, ACCOUNT_INACTIVE, MANUAL_CERT, …) are unchanged. `util/ErrorTexts.safeMessage` for controller catch blocks — never return `e.getMessage()` to a client.
  - **Client:** `utils/errorMessages.js` maps network / offline / timeout / abort / non-JSON / every HTTP status to a TR+EN message with a next step, keeps a specific server message, replaces technical text; network failures reject with `ApiError` (`status 0`, `NETWORK_ERROR`/`OFFLINE`). Status, code and request id ride as a non-enumerable `errorInfo`; `ui/ErrorDetails` ("Teknik ayrıntı") appears in Toast, AlertBanner and StatusBlock danger tones; `toast.error(msg, { duration, details })`. In a `catch`, prefer `e?.message || t('…')` over a bare `t('…')` (the message is already friendly).
  - **Gate:** `errorMessageQuality.test.js` — error-type i18n keys (TR + EN) must not be a vague phrase and must be ≥ 25 chars; the exception list may only shrink.
  - **Hand-written validation (2026-10-08):**
    - Map-body endpoints throw `GlobalExceptionHandler.FieldValidationException(field, Msg.t(...))`, which returns 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` + `fields` + `field`. It is an `IllegalArgumentException`, so old catch blocks still work.
    - A wrong JSON type (`ClassCastException`) returns 400, not 500.
    - On edit, a value that is unchanged from the stored one is exempt (old records stay editable).
    - k6 env names go through `ScriptedEnvPolicy`: save rejects bad names; at runtime denylisted names are dropped with a WARN.
- **Client timeouts (2026-10-08):**
  - `request()` gives GET/HEAD a 90 s default (`DEFAULT_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`); writes stay unbounded.
  - Pass `timeoutMs: 0` only for a GET that legitimately runs long (live checks).
  - `fetchWithTimeout` links the caller's own abort signal instead of overriding it.
- **Database health for external monitors (2026-10-08):**
  - `GET /api/public/health/db` (PUBLIC, `DatabaseHealthService`) checks connection, `SELECT 1`, PG writability, Hikari pool and schema patches.
  - UP/DEGRADED → 200, DOWN → 503. It is 4 s bounded and cached 5 s per pod.
  - It never returns host / db / user / exception text. `/health` (Actuator) is unchanged.
- **HSTS detail (2026-10-08):**
  - "Şimdi kontrol et" stores `latest_checks.hsts_policy` (raw header, max-age, includeSubDomains, preload, redirect) via `CertificateAppLayerProbe.policyJson`.
  - Health row values: max-age < 180 days → `hstsShortMaxAge`; max-age=0 → `hstsDisabled`; no max-age → `hstsInvalid`.
  - The row always expands into `certhealth/HstsExplainer` (`hstsModel.js` uses the same thresholds).
  - Alarm history shows a monitor-type badge (`AlertMonitorTypeBadge`, `monitorTypeOfAlert`); list rows carry `cert_source=MANUAL` for uploaded certs.
- **Paused certificate cards on the Dashboard (2026-10-08, user request).**
  - **Data:** paused (`active=false`) inventory rows come from a SEPARATE `GET /api/certificates/paused` (`CertificateService.getPausedForTeams`, same team scope as `/certificates`, uncached, every row `paused: true`). Do NOT add them to `getAllLatest`: that list also feeds Stats, Warnings, counters and team summaries.
  - **Rows:** a paused row whose domain also has an active row is dropped. A never-checked paused row is returned from inventory fields only.
  - **UI:**
    - App merges paused rows into the card list only (`pausedOnly` / `dashAll`); `certs` stays active-only.
    - Paused cards sort LAST in every sort. A stats-card filter never matches them.
    - The "İzleme" pill (`activity`: all | active | paused, with counts) filters them.
  - **Card look:** `certTone` → `paused`, while the text still uses `baseTone`. The card shows a dashed border, a "Pasif" badge with a pause icon and the `cert-paused-note` strip, plus `data-paused`.
  - **Card actions:** no Check now / renewal shortcuts; the "how to resume" sentence appears only with Edit.
  - **Gates:** `CertificateServicePausedTest`, `CertificateControllerTest` paused cases, `PausedCertCards.test.jsx`, `DashboardPausedCards.app.test.jsx`, `e2e/dashboard-paused.spec.js`.
- **All-certificates Trust column: filter + reason (2026-10-09, user request).** The column verdict (`trustOf`: ok / partial / unknown / bad + issues chain/untrusted/revoked) is mirrored server-side by `CertTrustVerdict`; `filter_trust` + facet `trust` + CSV use it, so the filter offers exactly what the column shows (`trustFilterOptions`, URL `c_tr`). Keep the two rules identical — `CertTrustVerdictTest` and `certTableModel.test.js` share one truth table. `certtable/TrustBadge` is a tap-to-open Popover (three checks via `trustChecks`, revocation reason via `revocationInfo` + `hlth.revReason.*`); trigger and content stop propagation so the row does not open. "Insecure only" (`filter_insecure`, includes hostname mismatch) is a separate toolbar toggle. Gates: `CertificatesTable.trust.test.jsx`, `e2e/cert-trust-filter.spec.js` (390/768/1280).
- **Shared-certificate window: team/group view + PDF/Excel (2026-10-08, user request).** The rich card chip "{0} alan adında ortak" opens `SharedCertificateModal`.
  - **Views:** List / By team / By group. Sections are A→Z (Turkish collation), unassigned last, with a domain count. The table has a Group column.
  - **Single source:** `sharedcert/sharedCertModel.js` (`groupPeers`, `exportTable`, `certInfoRows`) feeds the window, the PDF and the Excel, so the export follows the selected view.
  - **Export module:** `sharedcert/sharedCertExport.js` is lazy-loaded.
    - PDF: A4 landscape, jsPDF + autotable, Roboto, brand header.
    - Excel: real `.xlsx` from `utils/xlsxWriter.js`, a dependency-free OOXML writer on `fflate`. Inline strings (no formulas), bold header, frozen header + autoFilter, Turkish-aware sheet-name dedupe.
  - **Reuse:** `buildXlsx` is the project's Excel writer — reuse it instead of adding SheetJS.
  - **No backend change:** `/api/certificates/shared` already returns `group_name`.
  - **Gates:** `xlsxWriter.test.js`, `sharedCertModel.test.js`, `SharedCertificateViews.test.jsx`, `e2e/shared-cert-export.spec.js` (real download, unzip and `%PDF` check).
- **Load time, stale responses, logout hygiene (2026-10-09, user request: "kod seviyesinde bug, performans, mweb").**
  - **Lazy chunks:** the Markdown editor (`ui/mdEditor/LazyMdEditor`, `@uiw/react-md-editor/nohighlight`; the inventory editor uses `/common`), the HelpDrawer body + whitepaper, AlertNoisePanel, CommandPalette (`CommandPaletteLauncher` catches Ctrl+K while it loads), ShortcutsDialog, VersionPopover, SysMaintExtendDialog and the CertificateModal Alarm / Diagnostics / Inventory tabs load on first use. `lazyChunks.test.js` fails if a static import comes back. `IssueReportModal` stays eager (ErrorBoundary needs it).
  - **App data:**
    - `resetSessionData()` runs on every in-tab logout (manual + idle). It clears cards, counters, open windows and filters, and sets `loadAliveRef=false`.
    - `/stats/teams` is fetched only on the Stats tab.
    - `/certificates/paused` is part of the first `loadData` wave, filtered through the deleted marks.
    - **Progressive waves (2026-10-09, user report: the dashboard spinner waited for the slowest request):** `loadData` = `runLoadWave(FULL_LOAD_WAVE)`; each source (`loadSources`: certs, stats, silent, paused, mailFail, extras) applies ITS response as soon as it lands, so cards render on `/certificates` without waiting for `card-extras`. Every wave gets a sequence number and each source remembers the newest applied wave (`acceptLoad`) — an older wave never overwrites newer data; `resetSessionData` raises `loadFloorRef` so a previous session's late responses are dropped. `paused` applies only after the same wave's `certs` settled. Never go back to awaiting the whole wave before rendering (`DashboardProgressiveLoad.app.test.jsx`).
    - **Server-side warm caches:** `CertificateCacheWarmer` recomputes `cert-latest` / `cert-stats` / `card-extras` on one background thread at `ApplicationReadyEvent` and 2 s after `CertificateService.evictAllCaches()` (which publishes `CachesEvictedEvent`); bursts coalesce, no schedule/loop (`site.monitor.cache.warm-enabled`). The card "last alarm" strip uses `AlertEventRepository.findLatestForActiveInventoryDomains` (LATERAL per active inventory domain; old full-history query is the fallback).
    - **GET retry:** `api/client.js` `request()` retries a GET/HEAD ONCE after 400 ms when fetch itself rejected (network reset) — never for writes, HTTP statuses, abort, timeout or offline (`clientNetworkRetry.test.js`).
    - The idle countdown ticks only in `ui/InactivityCountdownText`. Never put a per-second `setState` in App.
  - **Sequence guards:** every list `load()` on the nine monitor pages, Storm history/analytics, Activity summary and the InventoryToolbar search debounce (reads filters from a ref) drop late responses. Open-detail sync goes through `utils/monitorDetailSync` (`mergeSavedRow`, `reloadAndSyncDetail`).
  - **Other changes:**
    - The 7/24 banner calls `/api/noc/coverage?summary=1` (summary only, no `items`) and shares one in-flight request.
    - `/api/renewal-advice` rows carry `renewal_planned_at|_note` when a plan exists.
    - The phone fullscreen modal class lives in `ui/modalClasses.js` (`PHONE_FULLSCREEN`).
- **Activity page redesign (2026-10-11, user request).** `ActivityLog.jsx` keeps every function (atype/astatus/arange/aq URL filters, folding, groups, detail, go-to-monitor, CSV, pagination, 30 s silent refresh) and adds: `activity/ActivityTimeline` (stacked bars from the summary's additive `series` — hourly ≤ 48 h, Istanbul days otherwise, last 30 days when no range; live badge), type chips with counts (`type_counts`: every filter except type, status included; count is an `aria-describedby` OUTSIDE the button so the name stays the type), active-filter chips (types / range / query — no status chip, the KPI already shows it), chips folded behind "İzleme türü" when the filter card is < 640 px, and table ↔ cards by FEED width (`activityModel.pickLayout`, 720 px; jsdom falls back to `useIsMobile`). Names, targets and team badges wrap (`TeamBadge wrap`), never truncate. Backend: `ActivityController.summary` + `countByTypeFiltered` + native `countByHourAndStatus` (`GROUP BY 1, 2`). Gates: `ActivityControllerTest`, `ActivityQueriesPostgresTest`, `ActivityLog.test.jsx`, `ActivityLogRedesign.test.jsx`, `activityModel.test.js`, `e2e/activity.spec.js`.
  - **2026-10-12:** the time window is computed at LOAD time (`rangeToFromTo`; "today" starts at Istanbul midnight, `istanbulDayStartMs`), only a FILTER change clears the old summary (page change / Refresh keep it until the new one lands), a failed summary shows "—" / the timeline error state — never 0 — and hides the "Canlı" badge. Gate `ActivityTimeWindow.test.jsx`.
- **Manual-cert upload status (2026-10-08):** `components/manualcert/wizard/UploadProgress` + pure `uploadProgressModel` — stages read → extract in browser (ZIP n/N, PKCS#12 note) → send (REAL `XMLHttpRequest` upload % via `request()`'s `transport` hook; `api.manualCerts.analyze|create|createBatch|renew(…, { onProgress, signal })`, fetch path when no `onProgress`) → server analysis / save; one `aria-live` line; "Vazgeç" aborts the worker/XHR (a save can be cancelled only while uploading). Errors go through `manualCertErrors.js` (`describeUploadFailure`): field errors under the field, others in a banner with `ui/ErrorDetails`. Gates: `ManualCertUploadProgress.test.jsx`, `manualCertUploadProgress.test.js`, `manualCertsXhrClient.test.js`.
- **Form validation errors live next to the field (2026-09-30).** Never report a missing required field only as a toast. Use `useFormErrors(resetKey)` (`fe.check({ key: cond && msg })` in save, `{...fe.fieldProps('key')}` on the `FormField`/`FormSection`, `fe.clear('key')` in the field's onChange): the message renders under the field (`ui/Field` `error` + `name` → `data-field`), the page scrolls to and focuses the first invalid field (`utils/formErrors.focusFormError`). All nine monitor forms follow this.
- **Default card order on the nine monitor type pages (2026-10-01, product decision).** `utils/monitorSort.js` `sortMonitorsDefault(list, type)` is the single source: (1) problem cards first — red (`down`) then yellow (`warn`), mirroring each page's own `statusKey` (`STATUS_TIER`; paused never counts as a problem); (2) monitors WITH a group name, group A→Z, same group adjacent, name A→Z inside; (3) monitors without a group, name A→Z; ties by id. Collation is Turkish, case-insensitive, numeric (`Intl.Collator('tr', { sensitivity: 'base', numeric: true })`). Every page sorts its filtered list into `orderedMonitors` before `usePagination`; the server's order (name ASC; Port/DNS inventory-then-standalone with no ORDER BY) is NOT what the user sees. Domain is the exception (same-day user decision: "the user sees the fewest days left first"): its default is `days_asc` (expired first, unknown days last, ties by domain, not written to the URL); this shared rule is the `default` option of its selector. If a page's `statusKey` changes, change `STATUS_TIER` too — `monitorSort.test.js` pins every type. Tests must find cards by label, never by position.
- **Monitoring menu badges + overview page (2026-09-30).** `useOpenAlerts` polls `GET /api/me/open-alerts` (per-type open alert summary in the viewer's scope, `OpenAlertsSummaryService`); `NavAlertBadge` renders the count + hover peek and navigates to `?tab=alerthistory&src=<type>` (the `src` category filter maps to `MONITOR_ALERT_TYPES`). The Monitoring Overview page (`?tab=monitoring`, `MonitoringOverviewPage`) reads `GET /api/monitoring/overview` (`MonitoringOverviewService`; status per monitor = last check + open alarm, "stale" = 3× interval, min 10 min). Inventory-derived Port/DNS rows whose host is not in the ACTIVE inventory are skipped by the sweep and hidden by the type page, so the overview counts them as `paused` + `inventory_inactive` (never `stale` or `down`, never in success rates; `TypeSpec.unmonitored`) — the match MUST mirror the sweep exactly (raw, case-sensitive `activeDomains.contains(host)`; prod 2026-10-01 showed orphan rows as "gecikmiş"/"sorunlu" while the host's standalone twin was healthy; `standalone_twin` links it). The overview is memoized 30 s per scope (`site.monitor.monitoring-overview.cache-ms`); `?fresh=1` (Refresh button) bypasses at most every 5 s. KPI boxes open summary dialogs (`OverviewKpiDialog`, URL `mo_dlg`) whose "Listede süz" applies the table filter. KPI/type-card clicks scroll to the list anchor (`data-slot="mo-list-anchor"`). The four top sections (key figures, fleet health, team health, monitor types) live in ONE shadcn Accordion (`OverviewSections`, product decision 2026-10-01): only the key figures are open by default, a single strip button expands/collapses all, closed headers carry a tone tile + summary chips, the open set is in the URL (`mo_open`; default not written, empty = `none`, never in browser storage) and the contents render compact (`MonitorStatsBar dense`, `OverviewHealthCard`/`OverviewTeamsCard bare`); the monitor list stays outside the accordion. Radix unmounts closed content, so tests that touch health/team/type cards must expand first (`expandAll` in `MonitoringOverviewPage.test.jsx`). A new monitor type must be added to `MonitorTypeCatalog`, `MONITOR_ALERT_TYPES`, `TYPE_META` in the overview page and a `TypeSpec` in the service.
- **Delivery log views (2026-10-01 redesign).** Weekly availability email log = modal `health/WeeklyAvailLogsModal` (+ pure `weeklyLogModel.js`, detail `WeeklyAvailLogDetail` as a NESTED ModalShell — a shadcn Sheet would render under the shell's z-index 2000+); Webhook push log = full-page `PushLogView` (+ `admin/push/*`: model, health card, rows, Sheet detail). Both pick table vs cards by LIST-CONTAINER width (`hooks/useElementWidth`, jsdom = 0 → table; 720 / 760 px), tables are `table-fixed` with header widths so long team / monitor names truncate instead of pushing the action column out (e2e asserts the table container does not scroll at 1280). Tests find rows by `data-slot="wa-row"` / `pl-row`, never `tbody tr` (day header rows live in the same tbody); narrow-card branch is covered by `logViews.mobile.test.jsx` (width hook mocked).
- **Monitor page refresh countdown lives in the header chip (2026-10-02).** The 9 type pages pass `refreshEvery={REFRESH_INTERVAL} refreshResetKey={loadNonce}` to `MonitorPageHeader` (the chip counts itself); never put a per-second `setState` in a page again — it re-rendered the whole grid and any open detail every second (`monitorPages.countdown.test.jsx`). App-level polling (5 min data refresh, 60 s network status) uses `useVisibleInterval`; the full data refresh on tab return runs only when the interval has elapsed. The session ping keeps running in hidden tabs on purpose (single-session registry).
- **Status page (`?tab=status`, 2026-10-02, proposal 18).** `GET /api/status-page` (authenticated, never public) = services (team × `group_name` across the 9 types) with state operational/degraded/partial_outage/major_outage/maintenance/no_data, active/resolved incidents, maintenance windows, 7-day availability from the daily rollup. Org-wide base memoized 30 s; per-viewer filtering on cached data. Incident and maintenance ROWS follow the existing read rules (`IncidentController.canReadIncident`, `MaintenanceController.canSeeWindow` + `incidents.view` / `maintenance.view`); out-of-scope items appear only as `*_hidden` counts — never widen visibility on this page. No URLs/hosts/errors in the payload (leak test).
- **User preferences, favourites, landing tab, saved views (2026-10-02, proposal 23).** `user_preferences` (one JSON doc per user, `GET/PUT /api/me/preferences`, whitelisted keys `favorites|landingTab|savedViews|local|dashboard`, 64 KB cap). `hooks/useUserPrefs.js` mirrors a WHITELIST of localStorage keys (`userPrefsModel.js` = `UserPreferencesService.LOCAL_KEYS`, a test compares them) by wrapping `Storage.prototype.setItem/removeItem` while a session is active: server wins on load, local-only values are uploaded once (not for a different previous owner), writes are debounced to one PUT. Language/drafts/tour/inbox sets/density are deliberately NOT synced; the THEME IS synced (2026-10-05, user decision — see Themes). Favourites = star in the shared `MonitorCardTop` + detail header, Monitoring Overview "Favoriler" view (`mo_fav=1`), palette group; they never change the default card order. Landing tab applies only when the URL has no params. `ui/SavedViewsMenu` stores page-state URL params per list (`VIEW_SPECS`); applying remounts the tab.
- **Menu names of the two dashboards (2026-10-13, user report: "Dashboard / My dashboard kafa karışıklığına sebep oluyor"; decision: names only, places unchanged).** `?tab=dashboard` = "Sertifika Panosu" / "Certificate dashboard" (it shows certificate and domain cards only), `?tab=mydash` = "Kişisel Panom" / "Personal dashboard" — the same family as the Monitoring group's "İzleme Panosu". Keys: `nav.dashboard|myDashboard`, `meta.tab.dashboard|mydash.title`, `app.dashTitle`, `mydash.title`, `landing.default`, `nav.goHome`, `notFound.inApp.dashboard`, the tour / shortcut texts and the help (`whitepaper*.md` §14.4 + tab table). Don't reintroduce a bare "Dashboard" / "Pano" label.
- **"Panom" — personal dashboard + TV / wall display mode (2026-10-11, user decision: a NEW tab; the existing Dashboard is unchanged).** `?tab=mydash`, `components/mydash/*`.
  - **Layout:** user preference key `dashboard` = `{v:1, widgets:[{id,type,size}]}` (`UserPreferencesService.normalizeDashboard`: ≤ 24 widgets, id `[a-z0-9-]`, type `[a-z_]`, size sm|md|lg; NO catalogue check so a widget added in a newer release survives an older client). `null` = default layout. Client: `useUserPrefs().dashboard / setDashboard`; unknown or not-permitted widgets are KEPT in the stored list but not drawn (edit operations work on the full list — `myDashModel.moveWidget` skips hidden ones).
  - **Catalogue (single source `myDashModel.WIDGET_CATALOG` + `widgets.jsx WIDGETS`, a test compares them):** noc_light (`alerts.read`), noc_uncalled (`alerts.read` + pseudo-permission `@noc_console`), alerts_open, monitor_health, cert_expiring, status_services, storms (`alerts.read`), oncall (`oncall.read`), activity, watchdog (`system_health.read`), data_quality (`data_quality.view`), maintenance (`maintenance.view`), incidents_open (`incidents.view`). `perm` may be an array (all required). Every widget reads an EXISTING endpoint (no new backend read path, scope/permissions unchanged) through `useWidgetData` (60 s, `useVisibleInterval`, sequence guard, last data kept on error with a "could not refresh" note). Each type at most once. Adding a widget: catalogue entry + component + TR/EN `mydash.w.<type>` / `mydash.wd.<type>`.
  - **Grid:** container queries (1 / 2 / 3 / 4 columns at @2xl / @4xl / @7xl — `columnsFor` uses the same thresholds), `grid-flow-row-dense` so mixed sizes leave no holes. Edit mode: native drag-and-drop on fine pointers, ← → buttons everywhere, size ToggleGroup, remove, catalogue dialog, "reset to default" (confirm → `setDashboard(null)`); every change is saved at once (debounced PUT) and announced in an `aria-live` line.
  - **List rows:** badge | name (full width, wraps) + detail line | short count badges (`Row` in `widgets.jsx`). Never put a name and a long secondary text in the SAME flex row — the TV e2e caught a service name squeezed to 14 px by a long team name.
  - **TV mode (`MyDashTv`, URL `md_tv=1`):** fixed full-screen overlay (body/html scroll locked, `scrollbar-gutter` off so it covers the whole width), clock, pages from `tvLayout.layoutTv` (2026-10-13, user request: "TV'de bir ekranda daha çok kutu, boyu ona göre, boş alanlar kullanılsın" — see the TV density bullet below; `paginate` / `rowsFor` stay for the old tests only), rotation 15/30/60/120 s or off (`sm.mydash.tv`, per browser), prev/next/pause (← → Space), Esc / Exit closes, controls and cursor hide after 4 s, Screen Wake Lock when supported, all pages stay mounted (no reload on rotation). If a page is still taller than the screen, `useFitScale` scales it down (`transform`, ≥ 0.6) — a wall display is never scrolled.
  - **TV density (2026-10-13):** `components/mydash/tvLayout.js` (pure). The measured main area + `sm.mydash.tv.density` (`comfortable` = the old 4 × 2 at 1920 × 1080, `compact` default, `dense`; header select, remembered per browser) give the maximum grid (min cell 420 × 440 / 300 × 260 / 240 × 240 px, ≤ 12 × 8). Widgets are packed 2-D first-fit into the FIRST open page with room (`packTv` — in-order cutting left a lone widget on a last page), at most 24. Colour panels take ONE cell in compact / dense (`tvSpan`; the user's size applies only in comfortable); list / grid widgets (`tvDetail`: `DETAIL_ONLY_TYPES` oncall / activity / service_grid / type_heatmap / team_matrix, or any widget while colour panels are off) span at least `TV_DETAIL_MIN_W` 520 × `TV_DETAIL_MIN_H` 300 px. Each page then picks its own grid (`fitPage`): colour-panel-only pages the biggest cells that fit (aspect 0.6–3), pages with list widgets keep the column count and use the fewest rows (list content does not grow with the cell — a 2-column grid stretched a list across the whole screen); `fillTrailingGaps` stretches a row's last one-row widget over the empty cells after it and the last row's widgets down into fully empty bottom rows; a single widget always gets the whole area (a portrait screen picked 1 × 2 and left half empty). Rows are `minmax(cellH, auto)`, so a taller list grows its row and `useFitScale` shrinks the page (≥ 0.6; the wall e2e requires ≥ 0.85). A widget laid out as a one-cell panel gets `frame.tvPanel` and ALWAYS draws a panel there (review 2026-10-13): an unpaintable tone (unknown), stale data → the unpainted big number, `stat == null` → "—" + the widget's own reason (`toneLabel`, else "no data"), a first-load failure → a short cell-scaled "couldn't load" (`mydash.tv.cellUnavailable`; the full sentence in `sr-only` / `title`), a refresh failure → one short chip (`mydash.tv.cellStale`, no age line) — never its list body, which grew the row, shrank the page and scrolled the wall during an outage. Such cards are size containers WITHOUT `overflow-hidden` (overflow must stay visible / testable): value / label / sub / title / chip use `cqmin` clamps (value ≥ 1.75 rem), tighter paddings, and the "updated" line appears only with an error. The page info and prev / pause / next / interval controls always reserve their space (`invisible` + `aria-hidden` with one page; the Tab trap skips them) — a header whose height followed the page count could loop layout ↔ page count. Changing density or colour panels restarts at page 1 (the page set changes). Gates: `tvLayout.test.js`, `WidgetCardTvCell.test.jsx`, `MyDashPage.test.jsx` (TV: 2 pages instead of 4 in jsdom, density switch), `e2e/mydash.spec.js` ("Panom TV — yoğunluk 1920×1080": 14 panels on ONE page in compact, 2 in comfortable, 1 in dense; no overflow / clipping, value ≥ 28 px, the page fills the screen; "küçük duvar" 1366 × 768 / 1280 × 720 / 1440 × 900 (alarm endpoint 500) in dense + compact with long-text panels: every element inside its card (rectangle check — `scrollHeight` cannot see content overlapping the footer), no list body in a panel cell, no scroll, fit ≥ 0.85; the e2e `clipped` helper skips `.sr-only`).
  - **Idle logout:** while TV mode is open `utils/tvMode.js` is set and App's inactivity warn/logout timers re-arm instead of firing (`AppTvIdle.app.test.jsx`). Server-side session rules (single session, passive, maintenance, admin kick) are unchanged.
  - **e2e trap:** a Playwright `fullPage` screenshot resets Chromium's touch emulation — take it at the END of a test, after the touch-target checks.
  - **Gates:** `myDashModel.test.js`, `MyDashPage.test.jsx`, `AppTvIdle.app.test.jsx`, `UserPreferencesServiceTest.dashboardLayout`, `e2e/mydash.spec.js` (390/768/1280 + 1920×1080 wall display fits without scrolling).
  - **Unknown is never green (2026-10-12):** no services / unknown monitors / scan errors / a null response render muted or warning, never success; `alerts_open` needs `alerts.read`, `monitor_health` needs `monitoring.read` (a `visible:false` answer shows "no access"). TV mode is a focus-trapped `role=dialog` (focus restored, fullscreen exited on close) and the grid is not rendered under it. Preferences are re-read on tab focus / visibility (at most every 30 s) and AT ONCE when another tab saved (`useUserPrefs` writes the `sm.prefs.rev` stamp after every successful PUT; the `storage` event triggers a re-read; a re-read is dropped if this tab wrote meanwhile (`generation()`), and never overwrites a local value another tab changed). Known limit (review round 4): if this tab is busy saving while another tab saves a whole-value key (`dashboard`, `favorites`, `savedViews`) within about a second, one of the two edits can be lost — a real fix needs a server-side revision check (409 on mismatch). Permissions: `usePermissions().failed` is set when the first snapshot can't be read — Panom shows an error with retry instead of loading forever; a response that lands after logout / user switch is dropped (sequence guard). The layout is never edited / saved before the permission snapshot arrived (`usePermissions().loaded`, false until the first `/api/me/permissions` answer; without it the default layout would be saved filtered by an empty snapshot) — the page shows loading instead of "empty". The watchdog widget is green only for `UP`; never evaluated → muted "not evaluated".
  - **Clickable widgets (2026-10-13, user request: "Kişisel Panom'dan hızlıca tıklayıp geçebilmeliyim"):** numbers, tiles and rows open the filtered screen or the record through `widgetParts.linkTo(frame, tab, params)` (→ `navigateTo`); in edit mode and TV mode they stay plain text (no accidental navigation). Targets: `alerthistory` `level` / `ack=unack` / `alert=<id>`, `monitoring` `mo_status` / `mo_type`, `all` `c_win` / `c_st`, `dashboard` `domain` + `open=cert`, `storms` `sf_team`, `dataquality` `dq_team`, activity rows via `utils/activityTarget.js` (shared with the Activity page). A count that mixes buckets the target filter cannot express links unfiltered (the "Warning" tile when level-less alarms exist). Clickable rows render only phrasing content (`span`, no `p`/`div` inside the button). A link button passes `data-slot` only when it has one (`widgetParts.slotAttr`): `data-slot={undefined}` wipes shadcn's `data-slot="button"`, and the project's border reset is `[data-slot] { border: 0 }` — without it the browser's 2 px outset border shows. Gate: `MyDashPage.test.jsx`, `MyDashNocWidgets.test.jsx`; touch targets + no bordered link in `e2e/mydash.spec.js`.
  - **24/7 command widgets + colour panel view (2026-10-13, user request: "7/24 ekibi önünde olsun; sorun varsa kırmızı, uyarı turuncu, yoksa yeşil; Grafana tarzı"):** `components/mydash/nocWidgets.jsx`, shared parts and fetchers in `widgetParts.jsx`.
    - **Status light** (`noc_light`): `/api/me/open-alerts` → critical/high open → red, only warning/other → amber, none → green, `visible:false` → grey (no link). Oldest open alarm from the additive `oldest_open_at` / `oldest_severe_at` (MIN(createdAt) in the SAME grouped query `countOpenByTypeLevelAck`, 5th column; absent when nothing is open; a 4-column row is tolerated).
    - **Awaiting a call** (`noc_uncalled`): `/api/noc/console?window=24h&noc=sent&called=no&state=open&size=50&page=0`; the console sorts these rows NEWEST first, so when `total` > rows read the widget also reads the LAST page (the oldest waiting alarms live there; a failed second read = failure state, never a colour from half the data) and de-duplicates by id (`fetchNocUncalled`, review 2026-10-13). Red when one waited ≥ `NOC_CALL_SLA_MIN` (15, client rule — the server has no call-time setting) since `noc.sent_at`, amber otherwise, green at 0; overdue is a lower bound ("N+") when rows were skipped; `truncated` (console base over 1000 rows, the oldest dropped first) = `partial` → never green, a "list incomplete" note. The header "open page" link passes the same console params (`WidgetCard tabParams`) — a non-operator global viewer would otherwise land on Coverage. Visible only with `nocConsole` (App: `noc.operator || globalViewer`, the same rule as `NocPage`); rows open the console with `n_view=console&n_cnoc=sent&n_ccall=no&n_cst=open&n_cq=<domain>`.
    - **Maintenance / open incidents** read the memoized `/api/status-page`. `StatusPageService` now flags `maintenance.unavailable` / `incidents.active_unavailable` when the read failed (it used to return empty lists = "none") — the widgets show the failure state, never green. Colour and counts come from VISIBLE records only (maintenance: total − hidden, the same rule as incidents): active maintenance → amber; visible critical/high incident → red, other open → amber; other teams' records only as `*_hidden` counts, and then the empty text is "nothing you can see" (`mydash.maint|inc.noneVisible`), never "none" (also in the header label and the colour panel's sub line, which carries the hidden count); "Monitors in maintenance" sums only services with `monitors_visible`; the console widget treats a list that changed between its two reads as `truncated`. The Status page tab uses the same flags (`sp.inc.unavailable`, `sp.maint.unavailable`; no count badge on an unreadable section). On a colour panel the "could not refresh" note is a dark chip in the panel's text colour (amber text was unreadable on red / green).
    - **Colour panel** (`style: 'stat'` per widget, `UserPreferencesService.WIDGET_STYLES`; unknown value dropped, not rejected): `WidgetCard` paints the whole card (`STAT_SURFACE`, fixed palette with ≥ 4.5:1 text) and shows the widget's `stat` headline. Only when the data is valid — failed / stale / muted cards are never painted. A widget opts in by passing `stat` (null = supported but nothing to show now; oncall / activity pass nothing → no toggle). TV mode paints every capable widget by default (`sm.mydash.tv` `panels: 'stat'|'detail'`, toggle "Renkli paneller").
    - **24/7 command layout** (`NOC_PRESET`, `presetLayout`): applied from the add-widget dialog after a confirm; widgets the user can't see are skipped, undrawable entries of the current layout are kept. The default layout is unchanged.
  - **Grafana-style grids (2026-10-13, `components/mydash/gridWidgets.jsx`):** `service_grid` (every status-page service as a SOLID colour tile, worst first; a problem tile whose monitors the viewer can see (`monitors_visible`) opens the Monitoring Overview with `mo_team` + `mo_status=down,stale`, others the Status page), `type_heatmap` (overview `types`, worst-status colour per type, types with no monitor hidden; click → `mo_type` (+ `mo_status`)), `team_matrix` (overview `monitors` grouped team × type, deleted / inventory-inactive skipped, worst team first, teamless last; table ≥ `@2xl` container, per-team chip cards below — no horizontal scroll; cell → `mo_team` + `mo_type` (+ `mo_status`); legend). Tone rule `healthTone`: down red, stale amber, unknown or paused-only grey, else green; a team's tone counts only cells with ACTIVE monitors (a paused-only type never greys a healthy team) and paused-only teams sort last and are ignored by the overall tone; inventory-inactive rows count as paused, exactly as the Overview lists them under `mo_status=paused`. Rows sort by tone first, teamless last only among equal tones (a red teamless row must not fall below the row cap). A cell's number = the rows its link lists (`matrixCellFocus`: down → stale → unknown → up → paused, the same `mo_status`). Cells keep their colour on hover (per-tone hover classes — the ghost Button's grey `hover:bg-accent` read as "unknown"). A service tile links to the Overview only with `monitoring.read`; an unreadable team name shows `mydash.matrix.teamUnnamed`, never a bare id. Every cell says its state in TEXT too (aria-label + visible count / text). The grids have NO colour-panel (`stat`) view — in TV they stay grids. A link `Cell` passes `data-slot` only via `slotAttr`, and its display class comes from the call site (a trailing `block` once overrode flex / grid). Gates: `MyDashGridWidgets.test.jsx`, `myDashModel.test.js`, `e2e/mydash.spec.js` (grids at 390/768/1280 + the 1920×1080 wall display).
  - **Colour panel widgets (2026-10-13, user request: "renkli panel adetlerini arttıralım"):** 14 single-figure widgets (`stat_*`; definitions `statDefs.js` — no imports, read by the catalogue AND `statModel.js`; readings + thresholds `statModel.statReading`; component `statWidgets.jsx`), always drawn as a colour panel (`WidgetCard forceStat`: no style toggle; an unpaintable tone — unknown / stale / no data — renders the big number unpainted, never green). Sources are existing endpoints (`alerts` / `overview` / `stats` / `status` / `noc`); widgets reading the same source share ONE in-flight request (`useWidgetData.sharedFetch`, keyed by the fetcher function — only in-flight, no response cache). Thresholds in one place: success rate ≥ 99 green / ≥ 95 amber, oldest critical/high alarm ≥ 30 min red, counts 0 green. "Kutu ekle" groups widgets vs colour panels (`catalog group: 'stat'`) and offers the "Renkli panel duvarı" preset (`STAT_PRESET`, all at sm). Review rules: "services in outage" is green only when EVERY service is operational (maintenance → info, no data → grey); monitor / team panels are grey while monitors are unmeasured (`unknown > 0`); the oldest-alarm link sorts `opened_asc` without a level filter (the oldest can be HIGH); `sharedFetch` lives in `utils/sharedFetch.js` — Refresh / fetcher change pass `fresh` (never join a hung request) and `App.resetSessionData` calls `clearSharedFetches()` (no cross-session join); panels carry a text tone label (`mydash.st.tone.*`) and `tabParams` for the header link. Widget TYPE names must match the server rule `[a-z_]{1,40}` — NO digits (`stat_certs_7d` would be dropped from saved layouts and the PUT rejected; gate `statModel.test.js`). Gates: `statModel.test.js`, `MyDashStatWidgets.test.jsx`, `e2e/mydash.spec.js` (colour panel wall at 390/768/1280).
    - **Gates:** `MyDashNocWidgets.test.jsx`, `myDashModel.test.js` (summaries, style, preset, TV panels), `OpenAlertsSummaryServiceTest.oldestOpenAt`, `PerformanceProjectionQueriesTest`, `StatusPageServiceTest.unreadableBlocksAreFlagged`, `UserPreferencesServiceTest.dashboardWidgetStyle`, `e2e/mydash.spec.js` (preset + colour panel at 390/768/1280, TV panels toggle).
- **Keyboard shortcuts + tour v2 (2026-10-02, proposal 24).** `utils/keyboardShortcuts.js` + `components/KeyboardShortcuts.jsx`: `?` cheat sheet, `/` focuses the page's `[data-page-search]` input, `g`+letter navigates (d m a s h i c) — only outside inputs/dialogs/menus, never with Ctrl/⌘/Alt; Ctrl+K/B/Enter unchanged. New search inputs that should be reachable by `/` get `data-page-search`. `TOUR_VERSION` 2: new steps carry `since: 2` so v1 finishers see only the "Yenilikler" card.
- **Shared helpers (2026-10-02, proposal 29).** Relative time → `utils/relativeTime.js`; CSV download → `utils/csvExport.downloadCsv` / `utils/csv.csvRows`; element width → `hooks/useElementWidth.js` (`useElementWidth()` = `[ref, clientWidth]`, `useElementWidthState()` = `[rounded rect width, setEl]`). Only byte-identical call sites were migrated (characterization tests hold the old bodies as oracles); the remaining look-alikes differ on purpose — check the characterization tests before "unifying" them. Free-text search on the nine monitor pages, Uptime and the certificate dashboard goes through `utils/monitorFilters.matchesSearchText` (2026-10-16, user report: Ping / Port / DNS / Keyword search did not find the monitor NAME shown on the card): `foldSearchText` folds Turkish case (İ I ı i) and accents (ş ç ğ ö ü), so "internet" finds "İnternet Şubesi"; every page searches the name it shows (gate `monitorNameSearch.test.jsx`, `monitorFilters.test.js`).
- **User editor (2026-10-02).** `admin/user-editor/UserEditor` is the ONLY user add/edit/view dialog. It serves the Users page and the team member card (the latter through the thin `UserEditModal` wrapper).
  - **Request body:** `userEditorModel.buildPayload` is byte-identical to the old forms (`userEditorModel.test.js` pins it).
  - **Tabs:** Hesap / Takımlar / Profil / Güvenlik are force-mounted (`hidden`), so the `data-field` and AD-hint hooks exist in every tab.
  - **Save:** disabled while there are no changes. Validation goes through `useFormErrors` and jumps to the first invalid tab. Closing with unsaved changes asks for confirmation.
  - **Guards:** self / last-admin / TEAM_ADMIN guards and the AD field locks are unchanged.
  - **Primary team:** it can be chosen only when adding a user. The update endpoint takes no explicit primary, so the editor only predicts it.
  - **Adding a new user field:** update `toForm`, `buildPayload`, `DIFF_FIELDS` and `FIELD_TAB` together.
- **Online users indicator (2026-10-02).** The green user-figure + count to the right of the sidebar logo (and in the phone top bar) reads `GET /api/presence/online` (`PresenceService`; every logged-in user may read it; counts and team names only, never people). "Online" means the users who have SiteMonitor OPEN right now (user decision: not "active in the last 2 minutes"). Technically it is `UserService.hasLiveSession`: an active account with a registered non-TERMINATED session and a fresh `lastSeenAt`. Logout, idle logout, admin kick and the passive/maintenance cuts clear the session at once. Closing the LAST open tab sends `navigator.sendBeacon('/api/session/leave')` (`utils/tabPresence.js`), which runs `UserService.markLeft`: it clears this session's `lastSeenAt` and the ping debounce entry, so a reload or another tab re-registers on its next ping. The `sm.tabs` localStorage registry decides "last tab"; a tab counts as alive for 3 min because browsers throttle hidden-tab timers. `site.monitor.session.active-window-seconds` (120 s) is only the fallback for crashes, sleep and network loss. Users are counted under their PRIMARY team (`app_users.team_id`; no team / deleted team → "Takımsız"), so the team rows add up to the total. One grouped query (`AppUserRepository.countOnlineByPrimaryTeam`), memoized org-wide for 10 s (`site.monitor.presence.cache-ms`). The client polls through ONE shared module-level poller (`hooks/usePresence.js`, 30 s, paused in hidden tabs), so several mounted indicators make one request per tick.
- **Sign-in page usage statistics (2026-10-04, user request).**
  - **Endpoint:** `GET /api/public-stats` is PUBLIC and `no-store`, with a 60 s server cache (single computation on an empty cache). It returns org-wide TOTALS only — never add names, domains or team names to this payload.
  - **Overview figures:** `healthy_monitors` / `active_monitors` / `total_monitors` / `checks_24h` / `failed_checks_24h` come from `MonitoringOverviewService.orgSummary()`. It uses the same computation and cache key as the global Monitoring Overview, so the sign-in "Sağlıklı" equals the overview tile. Check counts exclude certificate scans.
  - **Other figures:** `alerts_24h`, `teams`, `active_users` (active accounts), `online_users` (PresenceService) and `logins_24h` (distinct successful logins). Each figure is computed separately and is `null` on failure.
  - **`monitored_targets`:** now equals `active_monitors`. It used to be the storm denominator, which caused the 621 vs 688 mismatch; `StormService.totalActiveMonitors` stays storm-only.
  - **Usage switch:** GLOBAL_ONLY `site.monitor.public-stats.usage-enabled` (Branding page). When false the endpoint returns only `monitored_targets` + `availability_pct`.
  - **UI:** `components/login/UsageStats.jsx` (+ `usageStatsModel.js`). Desktop (≥ 1024 px): in the dark left panel UNDER the "Raporlama" pillar (user decision), with the form sticky in the viewport. Below 1024 px (pillars hidden): at the bottom of the page, under the form.
  - **Adding a field:** add it to `PublicStatsController.USAGE_FIELDS` and `USAGE_KEYS` together (`LoginUsageStats.test.jsx` compares them).
  - **Certificates (2026-10-05, user request):**
    - `checks_24h` = the overview's 24 h monitor checks (`monitor_checks_24h`) + 24 h certificate scans (`cert_checks_24h`, `certificate_checks`, failed = `status='error'`).
    - `failed_checks_24h` includes failed scans.
    - The second full-row tile is "İzlenen sertifika": `certificates` (active inventory), `certificates_ok` (latest status `valid`), `certificates_expiring_30d` (0..30 days), `certificates_expired` — ONE aggregate query on `certificate_inventory ⟕ latest_checks`.
    - Login "Koşum" therefore no longer equals the overview "Koşum (24 sa)" tile; the caption says how many certificate scans are included.
- Tests use `test-utils.jsx#render()` which wires up i18n + theme providers; mock the API client via `vi.mock('../api/client', ...)`. Never let a real `fetch` escape.

### Configuration
All config is `application.properties` keys overridden by env vars. Three profile files:
- `application.properties` — defaults (dev-leaning: in-memory session, dev CORS origins, `ddl-auto=update`).
- `application-prod.properties` — JDBC session, secure cookies, `same-site=strict`, log path `/var/log/site-monitor`.
- `application-local-pg.properties` — serves pre-built `frontend/dist` from `:8080` so you can run the whole stack on a single port without Vite.

`.env` is consumed by `docker-compose.yml` and `start-local.ps1`. Real values never committed — `.env.example` and `k8s/secret.example.yaml` are templates.

### Runtime schema patches (no Flyway/Liquibase)
Schema evolves through two mechanisms, not migrations:
1. `spring.jpa.hibernate.ddl-auto=update` handles new entities and new columns from `@Column` definitions.
2. **`SchedulerService.applySchemaPatches()`** runs idempotent `ALTER TABLE` / `CREATE TABLE` statements at startup via a `patch()` helper that swallows "column/table already exists" errors. This is how `certificate_inventory.use_proxy`, `escalation_contacts.team_id`, `TEXT` widenings, `scheduler_lock`, and the `port_*` / `dns_*` tables landed.

Rule: when you add a column an existing DB might not have, add a `patch()` line. Don't trust `ddl-auto` alone — it won't backfill defaults, rename columns, or widen types.
One-time DATA fixes never run on the startup path and never gate on "rows changed in this boot": they run in a background job under their own lock with a `schema_patch_markers` marker written only after full success, re-read INSIDE the lock; an unreadable DB dialect means "retry later", never "not PostgreSQL → mark done" (`DeferredIndexBuilder.postgresOrNull`). Example: `SchedulerService.rebuildPingRollupsOnce` (repair UPDATE + full ping roll-up rebuild, marker `2026-10-12-ping-rollup-rebuild-v2`, 120-min lock; review round 4: at most `PING_ROLLUP_REBUILD_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (24) failed attempts per pod, then one ERROR and the pod stops; a retry resumes from the failed weekly slice). `DeferredIndexBuilder` memoizes only a KNOWN dialect — an unreadable one runs neither the portable fallbacks nor the background build on that start (`memoKnown`).
**Indexes on big tables (2026-10-08): never a blocking `patch("CREATE INDEX …")`.**
- Add them to `service/schema/DeferredIndexBuilder.CATALOG`.
- On PostgreSQL they are built after `ACCEPTING_TRAFFIC` with `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, on an off-pool connection.
- One pod builds at a time (advisory lock `SMIDXBLD`). An INVALID leftover is dropped and rebuilt on the next start.
- An unreadable dialect at boot is never "not PostgreSQL": no fallback DDL runs and the build thread re-reads the dialect (`DIALECT_RETRIES` 5 × 30 s) before giving up until the next start; a known answer is memoised, an unknown one never is. With no memoised answer the first read also happens on the build thread (`KnownMemo.peek`), so the caller never waits for the pool's connection timeout.
- H2 runs the portable fallback through `patch()`.
- Never drop an index that `@Table(indexes)` still declares — ddl-auto recreates it.
3. **`SchemaPatchRunner`** (`service/schema`, 2026-10-02) executes every `patch()` — same idempotent behaviour, but a REAL failure on PostgreSQL is now a WARN + counted (expected "already exists / does not exist / duplicate" classes stay silent, classified by SQLSTATE so Turkish server messages work), the startup prints one summary line ("Şema yamaları: N uygulandı, M zaten uygulanmış, K başarısız"), and `sitemonitor.schema.patch.failed|applied|noop|locked` gauges expose it. The whole patch run is wrapped in a PostgreSQL session advisory lock (`LOCK_KEY` "SMSCHEMA", wait ≤ 60 s, then proceeds unlocked as before) so two pods never patch concurrently. The `postgres-it` suite (`SchemaPatchPostgresTest`) proves 0 failures on an empty PostgreSQL and on a second start, and that every index/column/constraint a patch names really exists.
4. **`SchemaDdlGuard`** (`service/schema`, 2026-10-09, user decision: "run only if needed, 20 s lock wait"). Statements that run on every boot are skipped when the change is already in the catalog: `ALTER COLUMN … TYPE TEXT|VARCHAR(n)`, `DROP NOT NULL`, `DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS`, `SET (reloptions)`, `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX [CONCURRENTLY] IF NOT EXISTS` (also used by the NOC patch classes). A statement that does run uses `SET lock_timeout = '20s'` … `RESET` on the same pooled connection (`CONCURRENTLY` excluded — a timed-out CIC would leave an INVALID index that `IF NOT EXISTS` skips forever); SQLSTATE 55P03 is a WARN, counted as failed with "sonraki açılışta yeniden denenecek". A new unconditional patch FORM must get a recognizer or be written conditionally — `SchemaDdlGuardPostgresTest.everyUnconditionalPatch_isInPlaceAfterBoot` scans the literal `patch("…")` calls and fails otherwise. Locally the postgres-it suite can run on a throwaway cluster (`initdb -U it -A trust` + `pg_ctl -o "-p 55432"` from the local PostgreSQL 16 binaries; delete it afterwards).

### Cert-check thread pool
`WebConfig` registers a `certCheckExecutor` (`ThreadPoolTaskExecutor`) shared by `CertificateCheckerService` / `PortCheckerService` / `DnsCheckerService` / `UptimeHttpCheckerService`. Sized by env:
- `EXECUTOR_CORE_SIZE` (default 20), `EXECUTOR_MAX_SIZE` (50), `EXECUTOR_QUEUE_CAPACITY` (5000). Prod Helm values mirror these (20/50/5000); the rejected-execution handler runs overflow on the caller thread and counts it in `WebConfig.CALLER_RUNS` (System Health → Task Queue).
- **Live tuning (2026-09-10):** the same three keys are in `AppSettingsCatalog` (group `executor`) and editable under Settings → General → Task Pool. `AppSettingsService.save` publishes `AppSettingsChangedEvent`; `ExecutorTuningService` applies core/max/queue to the `TunableThreadPoolTaskExecutor` bean immediately (queue is a `ResizableCapacityQueue`, so capacity changes without restart). Env values are only the boot fallback; a stored override is re-applied on `ApplicationReadyEvent`. Cross-field rule (`core ≤ max`, `queue ≥ 1`) is enforced at save time.
Bump these when inventory grows past a few hundred rows or you see queue backpressure in metrics.

### Other services in the codebase
Services worth knowing about beyond the ones already mentioned:
- **`PortCheckerService` / `DnsCheckerService` / `UptimeHttpCheckerService`** — sibling sweep services with their own scheduler entry points; results flow to the Uptime / Port / DNS tabs. `DnsCheckerService` emits `CHANGED` / `ROTATED` events when records flip — these surface as alerts.
- **Domain (alan adı) registration monitoring** — `DomainCheckerService` + `RdapDomainClient` (proxy-aware: IANA bootstrap → registry RDAP → rdap.org fallback, 429-retry, per-monitor timeout `site.monitor.domain.rdap-timeout-ms`) + `WhoisDomainClient` (port-43 fallback, env-gated, off behind a 443/80-only proxy). `DomainMonitor` → append-only `domain_checks`; a daily sweep (hourly tick + `checkDue` per-domain, `scheduler_lock`) raises `DOMAINMON_UNKNOWN/EXPIRY/STATUS/CHANGED` via `MonitoringOutageService`. Manual `POST /monitoring/domain/{id}/check` (and the diagnostic) also evaluate alarms via `SchedulerService.evaluateDomainAlarmsNow`. The **"Domain Kaydı / Registration"** modal tab (`GET /monitoring/domain/{id}/registration?live=`, perm `domain.registration.view`) shows registrar + IANA ID, important dates, nameservers, resolved A/AAAA IPs + reverse-DNS hostnames, EPP status (tooltipped), DNSSEC — the four new fields (`registrar_iana_id` / `dnssec` / `resolved_ips` / `hostnames`) parse from the same RDAP/WHOIS response + `DnsCheckerService` and are patched into `domain_checks` in `applySchemaPatches()`. Corporate SSL-inspection breaks RDAP-over-443 with PKIX unless its CA is in `site.monitor.trust.ca-bundle-pem` (`TrustEvaluator`, live-reload); admin tools `/api/admin/diagnostics/domain-expiry` (step trace) and `/proxy-ca-chain` (capture the proxy CA as paste-ready PEM) diagnose it. `.tr` has no public RDAP → needs WHOIS/port-43, which the proxy can't carry (firewall opening required).
- **`ChainValidationService`** — chain + OCSP (primary) + CRL (fallback) via BouncyCastle 1.81. CRL responses are Caffeine-cached (max 200 entries, `CRL_CACHE_TTL_HOURS`). Proxy-aware independently of the main cert check.
- **`GeoIpService`** — login/audit IP enrichment via `ip-api.com`, 1h cache. Powers the audit viewer's country/city columns.
- **`HttpMetricsService` + `HttpMetricsInterceptor` + `MetricsService`** — feed the Prometheus endpoint at `/metrics` (actuator base-path `/` + `path-mapping.prometheus=metrics`); `micrometer-registry-prometheus` is on the classpath. `/metrics` is unauthenticated on the main port — block it at the ingress/NetScaler (see `docs/RUNBOOK.md`).
- **`RememberMeService`** — token table `remember_me_tokens`, 7-day TTL, hourly cleanup task.
- **`ShutdownLogger`** — `@PreDestroy` hook that records the reason for JVM shutdown to the log; added because silent pod restarts were hard to diagnose.
- **`SqlPlaygroundService` / `SqlPlaygroundController`** — admin-only read-mostly SQL runner under `/api/admin/sql`. Audited.
- **`ExtendedHealthService`** — backs the System Health tab (heartbeat history, SMTP stats, DB metrics, scan staleness, scheduler-lock status).
- **`WebhookService`** — Teams / Slack delivery alongside email, 10s timeout, results logged to `notification_logs`.
- **`DbAnalyticsService`** — backs System Health → "Veritabanı" analytics (`GET /api/admin/system/db-analytics?days=1|7|30`). DB-wide top/slow SQL via **`pg_stat_statements`** (best-effort `CREATE EXTENSION` on `ApplicationReadyEvent`; falls back to the `sql_query_history` playground log when the extension isn't preloaded — branch on `summary.pgss`), plus `pg_stat_user_tables` (most-used tables + sizes) and `pg_stat_activity` (connections). `recent_queries` is the raw row list behind the "Sorgu"/"Ort. Süre" card drill-downs. Time-series bucketed in Java to Europe/Istanbul (hourly for 1-day, daily otherwise).
- **`UserActivityService`** — backs System Health → "Kullanıcı/Oturum". Aggregates `audit_logs` into login series, top users/sources, anomaly breakdown, and a 7×24 peak heatmap; lists active users from the single-session registry. Read access is global-admin **or** AUDIT (`requireSystemRead`), looser than the `requireAdmin` most admin endpoints use.
- **`AuditService`** (`@Async`) — writes every LOGIN / LOGIN_FAILED / LOGOUT / CRUD / SETTING event to `audit_logs` with IP+geo and a CSV `anomaly_flags` column (OFF_HOURS / UNUSUAL_IP / GEO_VELOCITY / BRUTE_FORCE / RATE_LIMITED). Detection windows are `site.monitor.audit.*` props.
- **`MonitoringOutageService`** — confirmation state machine for the uptime/port/DNS sweeps (alarm types ACCESSIBILITY / PORT_DOWN / DNS_FAILURE / DNS_CHANGED). Requires N consecutive confirmations before alerting to damp flapping; type-isolation keeps one signal type from masking another.
- **LDAP/AD** (`LdapSettingsService` / `LdapDirectoryService` / `LdapProvisioningService`, UI under `/api/admin/ldap`) — DB-persisted config with AES-GCM bind password and live reload (no restart); config, test-bind, attribute viewer, login (`AuthController.tryLdapLogin`) and provisioning (`LdapProvisioningService`) are live; group→role mappings are applied since 2026-10-11 only when `ldap_settings.role_mapping_enabled` is on (default off; `service/ldap/LdapRoleMapping`, locked role / last active ADMIN / bootstrap admin never changed); the "Kim ne olacak?" preview resolves CN rules at the naming-context root (the `DC=` suffix of the base DN, which is usually the USER search base) because login matches a `memberOf` CN anywhere, not only under the group search base. No JNDI dependency was added. **Per-field locks** (`LdapFieldLocks`, column `app_users.locked_fields`, wire `locked_field_keys`): an admin edit that REALLY changes an AD-sourced field of an LDAP user locks that field so provisioning/resync never overwrites it (blank ↔ null is not a change); only a global admin releases it via `POST /api/admin/users/{id}/field-unlock` (audit `USER_FIELD_UNLOCK`, scoped manager 403), and `LdapMembershipService.check()` lists every field as `fields[{key, ad, local, locked, differs}]` for the "Compare with AD" tab.
  **2026-10-12:** a settings save is applied to the CURRENT DB row (never the up-to-10-s-stale pod cache; first save starts from the form defaults) and the form sends only changed fields — the three role-mapping fields always together, so a rule set is enabled exactly as previewed. The manager path (provisioning a subordinate's manager from AD) never provisions, modifies or links a same-named LOCAL account (bootstrap admin included). Gates: `LdapSettingsServiceTest.saveAppliesToCurrentRowNotStaleCache`, `LdapProvisioningServiceTest.managerPathNeverTouchesLocalAccount`, `LdapRoleMappingSection.test.jsx`.
- **Weekly reports** (`WeeklyReportService`, `WeeklyReportReminderService`, `/api/weekly-reports`) — per-team DRAFT→PENDING→APPROVED state machine on ISO weeks; Friday 09:00 Europe/Istanbul reminder cron. Public **email-token approval links** intentionally bypass login.
- **Incidents** (`IncidentService`, `/api/incidents`) — SRE incident ledger with image attachments, trend rollups, and configurable status/priority/category options.
- **Postmortem template + action tracking (2026-10-11, user request).** `service/incident/*`, `IncidentPostmortemController`, `components/incidenthistory/postmortem/*`. The incident record itself is unchanged.
  - **Data:** `incident_postmortems` (one per incident, `@Version` → stale save 409 `CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION`; status DRAFT → IN_REVIEW → PUBLISHED, `published_at/by` stamped on publish, cleared when unpublished) with ten blameless sections (summary, impact, root_cause, trigger (`trigger_text` column), detection, resolution, went_well, went_wrong, lucky, lessons; ≤ 20 000 chars each). `incident_action_items` (≤ 50 per incident; kind PREVENT/DETECT/MITIGATE/PROCESS/OTHER, priority, status OPEN/IN_PROGRESS/DONE/CANCELLED, owner = an ACTIVE user (`owner_user_id`, in `UserRefWire.KEYS`; `owner_name` snapshot), due date `yyyy-MM-dd` Istanbul, `completed_at`, `reminded_on`). With no report the GET returns a template prefilled from the incident's description / business impact / RCA / resolution — nothing is written until Save. Section/status/kind lists are mirrored in `postmortemModel.js` (test reads the Java source).
  - **Gates (same as incident records):** read = `incidents.view` + `IncidentController.canReadIncident`; write = `incidents.manage/edit` + `inOwnIncidentScope` (the 7/24 read widening never writes). Exception: the action's OWNER may change ONLY its status (any other field → 403, never a silent partial save). The cross-incident list `GET /api/incidents/actions` (status open|done|all, mine, overdue, q) = read scope OR owner. Rows carry `mine` (computed server-side — the client never compares opaque ids). Audit `INCIDENT_POSTMORTEM_SAVE`, `INCIDENT_ACTION_ADD|UPDATE|DELETE`.
  - **Timeline:** built on read from existing rows only — incident stamps, the linked alarm (opened / first SENT notification + count / acknowledged / resolved / comments ≤ 50) ONLY with `alerts.read` and when the alarm's team is visible (teamless alarm = linked by the incident → visible), action added / done / cancelled, postmortem published. `deployment_history` is SiteMonitor's own rollouts, so it is deliberately NOT in the timeline.
  - **List badges:** `/api/incidents` rows gain `postmortem_status`, `actions_open|overdue|done|total` from TWO grouped queries per page (`IncidentPostmortemService.summaries`). Deleting an incident deletes its postmortem + actions (retention orphan rules `incident-postmortems-orphan` / `incident-action-items-orphan` back it up).
  - **Reminder:** `IncidentActionReminderService` daily 09:00 Istanbul (`site.monitor.incident.action-reminder-cron`, `…-enabled` true) under `scheduler_lock` `incident-action-reminder`: one mail per owner for actions due tomorrow, today, on the first overdue day, then weekly. Each item is claimed BEFORE sending (conditional UPDATE on `reminded_on`) → at most once across pods. Every decision is a `notification_logs` row (trigger `INCIDENT_ACTION`, alertEventId 0): SENT / FAILED / no address / passive user / system maintenance (muted → NO claim, retried next day).
  - **UI:** detail sheet card "Postmortem ve aksiyonlar" → `PostmortemModal` (tabs Report / Action items / Timeline, forceMounted; textarea editing, rendered markdown read-only, conflict banner + "load latest", Markdown download via `utils/csvExport.downloadText`); `ih_view=actions` switch = `ActionItemsView` (KPI tiles open / overdue / mine, filters `ih_astatus|ih_amine|ih_aover|ih_aq`, owner status change, "Open incident"). Adding a section / status / kind: Java list + `postmortemModel.js` + TR/EN `pm.*`.
